# Fetcher verification log

## 2026-11-24 Wave 2 rechecks

- **Connecticut and Virginia.** The direct and same-day embed-header GETs both returned Connecticut's November 24 High report. The beta had reported a missing report cell. DEEP's agency page embeds the same report, so it adds no independent rating. The retry still requires today's report and explicit geography. Virginia DWR's Rapidan parser accepts the publisher's “Fires Prohibited” heading before the unchanged terms. [DEEP report](https://www.depdata.ct.gov/forestry/forestfire/firerpt.cshtml) · [Rapidan WMA](https://dwr.virginia.gov/wma/rapidan/)
- **Wisconsin, Alaska and Gallatin.** WisBURN's JSON and CSV both contained only November 23 or older records on November 24. Alaska's ArcGIS data edit was September 15. Gallatin's latest dated table was November 20, and its permit portal displayed closures without a decision date. These sources report a failed check and wait for fresh dated evidence. Other independently verified rules can still apply. [WisBURN and hotline](https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/forestryapps/burnrestriction) · [Alaska area offices](https://dnr.alaska.gov/burn/fireareas) · [Gallatin status and permit office](https://www.readygallatin.com/residents/open-burning/)
- **Oregon and Illinois.** Both LRAPA grids recovered and parsed with their Pacific dates, returning seven yard-debris decisions and no wood-heating restrictions during the recheck. WTYE's filtered REST response replayed the Crawford County ban. The station's public WordPress post-ID URL redirected to the same report, so the adapter can use it when a same-station permalink changes format. [LRAPA outdoor burning](https://www.lrapa-or.gov/air-quality-protection/burning-overview/outdoor-burning/#current-restrictions) · [Crawford announcement](https://www.wtyefm.com/?p=257114)
- **Tennessee and New Hampshire.** The Tennessee permit guidance returned 403. New Hampshire's statute returned 520 from beta and 200 on the direct recheck. Both can publish their previously reviewed statutory baseline during transport failures, subject to its local windows and the pinned 180-day review window. A reachable change to the rule suspends the fallback. Coverage and county details distinguish the last live check from the statutory review. [Tennessee statutes and scope](https://www.tn.gov/tnwildlandfire/prevention/safe-debris-burning/tn-wildfire-fire-laws.html) · [New Hampshire RSA 227-L:17](https://gc.nh.gov/rsa/html/XIX-A/227-L/227-L-17.htm)
- **Texas issuer checks.** Archer's November 24 status page linked the October 6 signed rescission and the PDF matched the reviewed digest. Bexar's November 3 agency notice and the Fire Marshal's November 24 advisory both passed parser validation. If the Texas forestry feed continues to report the rescinded order, the county result shows both dated sources as a conflict. [Archer signed rescission](https://www.co.archer.tx.us/upload/page/1317/2026/20261006_BURN_BAN_LIFTED.pdf) · [Bexar county notice](https://nextdoor.com/agency-post/tx/san-antonio/bexar-county-commissioners/burn-ban-lifted-in-unincorporated-bexar-county-523172242/) · [Bexar Fire Marshal](https://www.bexar.org/630/Fire-Marshal)

## 2026-11-24 Michigan (MI)

- **Restrictions.** The Michigan DNR debris-burning permit map covers 49 northern Lower Peninsula and Upper Peninsula counties. At 00:00 Eastern, it showed permit restrictions or local issuance in 10 counties (Bay, Charlevoix, Clare, Grand Traverse, Houghton, Leelanau, Mackinac, Marquette, Muskegon and Roscommon). It showed permit availability with precautions in 39 and referred the other 34 to local issuers. The site reports 10 restricted, 73 unknown, zero general bans and zero all-clears. [MapServer layer](https://gisp.mcgi.state.mi.us/arcgis/rest/services/DNR/Burn_Permits/MapServer/1), [DNR permit map](https://www2.dnr.state.mi.us/burnpermits/).
- **ArcGIS MapServer.** One hourly attribute GET uses `lib/arcgis.ts`, `lib/dates.ts` and `lib/http.ts`. The ArcGIS helper validates attribute responses and decodes the map's Eastern wall-clock refresh time. Rows expire after two hours or at the next Eastern midnight, whichever comes first. [Fixture and request](../src/worker/sources/mi/fixtures/README.md).
- **Township details.** Use the county dropdown for the exact rule or local contact. The ASP.NET WebForms lookup takes a postback for each county and shows no decision date. Spot checks found a local referral in Houghton's Torch Lake Township, limits on what can be burned in Marquette Township, and both local referrals and a township issuing no permits in Bay. Macomb Township has a separate permit application and asks residents to call before burning. [DNR daily lookup](https://www2.dnr.state.mi.us/burnpermits/), [Macomb Township fire department](https://www.macomb-mi.gov/196/Burn-Permits).
- **Rules and access.** The DNR issues debris-burning permits in the north. Local fire departments or governments issue them in the south. The state fire marshal and local fire chiefs can impose emergency restrictions. The governor can restrict fire use on or near forest lands by proclamation. No current statewide proclamation surfaced. The forest-land permit requirement depends on snow cover, and local ordinances and EGLE air quality rules also apply. Direct curl to the DNR permit guide, April lookup release, fire alert, EGLE brochure and November safety article hit Akamai 403. Indexed official copies confirm the lookup workflow, restrictions in some areas without county decisions, the snow-cover rule and material limits. [DNR lookup release](https://www.michigan.gov/dnr/about/newsroom/releases/2026/04/07/need-a-burn-permit-streamlined-web-page-is-easier-to-use), [DNR fire alert](https://www.michigan.gov/dnr/firealert), [EGLE brochure](https://www.michigan.gov/-/media/Project/Websites/egle/Documents/Programs/AQD/Open-Burning/Open-Burning-Brochure.pdf?rev=a4d5e695bac943bf8b8d42ec78eddbcd), [EGLE November guidance](https://www.michigan.gov/egle/newsroom/mi-environment/2026/11/04/safe-burning), [Forest Fire Act](https://www.legislature.mi.gov/documents/mcl/pdf/mcl-451-1994-III-2-4-FOREST-FIRES-515.pdf), [local emergency authority](https://legislature.mi.gov/Laws/MCL?objectName=MCL-29-7A).
- **Verification.** Local cron and delayed merge reported 49 DNR permit records. Marquette was restricted, Keweenaw was unknown with a DNR permit note, and Macomb was unknown with a local referral. All 87 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. The Michigan fixture parsed in 0.13 ms p95. The final link check had 73 OK, 10 blocked and zero failed after transient GIS and Tennessee errors on earlier runs.

## 2026-11-24 New Jersey (NJ)

- **Restrictions.** Around 00:15 Eastern, the Forest Fire Service's division rows all reported `N` for campfire stages, agricultural restrictions and frost exceptions. Divisions A and C had edits within 48 hours and supplied scoped reports for 18 counties, including parts of Mercer and Middlesex. Division B last changed November 18 and was too old to use. The county layer had 21 inactive placeholders, including a legacy Gloucester agricultural `Y` dated June 2023. The site reports 21 unknown, zero restricted, zero bans and zero all-clears. [Official dashboard](https://www.arcgis.com/apps/dashboards/ab223d9403e94560a60b31b4141cc46e), [fixtures and request URLs](../src/worker/sources/nj/fixtures/README.md).
- **ArcGIS FeatureServer.** The hourly fetch uses `lib/arcgis.ts` and `lib/http.ts` for both the division and county attribute queries. A new shared UTC edit-window helper expires each report from its own edit timestamp. County reports take precedence when the dashboard marks them active. Stale reports and unknown flags leave the affected status unconfirmed.
- **Rules and access.** Forest Fire Service wardens issue permits and can restrict campfires in wooded areas or agricultural burning. The State Firewarden can suspend permits, and local fire officials enforce local rules and the fire code. Start with the dashboard for the active stage and county overrides. Check the permit pages and the local fire official for the address. The state wildfire hub, dashboard wrapper and permit pages returned Imperva challenges from curl. The original `nj.gov` dashboard link forwards to the wrapper by HTML redirect. The direct ArcGIS dashboard and both data layers were reachable. Hopewell's PDF is a standing permit and division guide. It carries no current order. [Campfire permits and stages](https://dep.nj.gov/parksandforests/wildfire/recreational/), [agricultural permits](https://dep.nj.gov/parksandforests/wildfire/agricultural/), [Hopewell permit guide](https://www.hopewelltwp.org/DocumentCenter/View/249/Open-Burning-Regulations-PDF).
- **Verification.** After the local cron and delayed merge, the coverage API returned 18 scoped county reports. All 21 county endpoints reported unknown. Mercer preserved its Division A boundary, and Ocean had no fresh source row. All 94 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. The NJ fixtures parsed and mapped in 0.079 ms p95. The link checker reported 75 OK, 11 blocked and zero failed.

## 2026-11-24 Wisconsin (WI)

- **Restrictions.** The 05:22 UTC capture was 11:22 p.m. Central on Nov. 23. The DNR export contained 31 permit-limit records (4 p.m. to midnight, with material and size limits), 12 seasonal permit exceptions and 29 local referrals. Of the 43 DNR-area records, 20 carried same-day edits. The site reports 13 restricted counties, 59 unknown, zero countywide bans and zero all-clears. Seven unknown counties retain a seasonal permit note. Vilas limits depend on snow cover. The Oconto record distinguishes northern and southern townships and was dated Nov. 22. The Nov. 23 rows expire at Central midnight before the Nov. 24 DNR check window. [Fixture and request](../src/worker/sources/wi/fixtures/README.md).
- **ASP.NET MVC JSON feed.** A single GET at 9 a.m., noon and 4 p.m. Central uses `lib/http.ts`, `lib/feeds.ts`, `lib/dates.ts` and `lib/html.ts`. The shared .NET date parser reads the UTC edit instant. All 72 texts and timestamps matched the CSV and ArcGIS exports. The ArcGIS `NO_BURN_FLAG` is also set on routine permit limits. The adapter reads the explicit permit wording and omits older edits, local referrals and unrecognized values. All Wisconsin candidates in the inventory were reachable. [DNR JSON](https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/forestryapps/burnrestriction/json/), [CSV](https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/forestryapps/burnrestriction/csv/), [map layer](https://dnrmaps.wi.gov/arcgis/rest/services/FR_WIS_BURN/FR_WIS_BURN_MAP_EXT/MapServer/13).
- **Rules and follow-up.** In intensive areas, the DNR generally requires a permit year-round when the ground lacks complete snow cover. Extensive areas generally require one January through May, and the DNR can issue emergency orders outside that period. Incorporated cities, villages and cooperative areas can set their own rules. The 2024 lift notice and May 2026 fire danger release are historical. The permit application, DNR materials guidance and Madison forms and guide explain standing rules. Check the address in WisBURN or call 1-888-947-2876 after 9 a.m. Central, then confirm local rules with the fire department. [WisBURN map](https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/wisburn/), [DNR rule summary](https://dnr.wisconsin.gov/topic/OpenBurning/Summary.html), [Madison rules](https://www.cityofmadison.com/fire/permits-inspections/licenses-permits/burn).
- **Verification.** The local cron and delayed merge reported 20 scoped county records. All 72 county endpoints agreed with the fixture counts. Vilas was restricted, Dane carried a seasonal note, and Kenosha had no DNR decision. All 101 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Wisconsin parsed in 0.541 ms p95 and the current-source merge and encode took 4.522 ms p95. The existing 51-area projection still crossed the review threshold at 10.015 ms p95. All Wisconsin links passed. The final sitewide link check had 79 OK, nine blocked and two failures. Utah State Parks redirected to a staging page that returned HTTP 503, and Tennessee's burn-ban page reset the connection.

## 2026-11-24 Minnesota (MN)

- **Restrictions.** At 05:31 UTC on Nov. 24 (11:31 p.m. Central on Nov. 23), all 92 DNR areas across 87 counties showed code 2, permits required. Only northern St. Louis County had an area update within 48 hours, on Nov. 23 at noon Central. The site reports one restricted county with partial coverage, 86 unknown, zero countywide bans and zero all-clears. [DNR map and legend](https://www.dnr.state.mn.us/forestry/fire/firerating_restrictions.html), [fixture and request](../src/worker/sources/mn/fixtures/README.md).
- **MapServer CGI / JSON.** The hourly GET uses `lib/http.ts`, `lib/feeds.ts` and `lib/dates.ts`. The shared report window also serves the ArcGIS edit-date helper. The adapter uses each area's timestamp and preserves split permit areas. The public map's overall date comes from the visitor's clock. The downloadable map was dated Nov. 23, and the permit directory also listed permits required for 92 areas. Confirm with the DNR whether older area timestamps represent unchanged decisions that the agency still verifies daily. [CGI feed](https://maps.dnr.state.mn.us/cgi-bin/mapserv/burning_restrictions?mode=nquery), [county permit directory](https://apps.dnr.state.mn.us/burning-permits/admin_areas).
- **Authority and access.** The DNR commissioner can suspend permits and restrict open fires by order. Authorized fire wardens and other agents issue permits, and eligible counties can adopt general permits. The May spring permit suspension and July Class IV order were historical, and the August notice lifted the last elevated state restrictions. Spring dates depend on conditions. Standing permit exceptions include qualifying campfires and continuous snow cover of at least three inches. All six inventory URLs responded. Use the map and permit office for the address, then check municipal, Tribal or federal rules as applicable. Superior National Forest also lists October wildfire area closures. [Minnesota statutes](https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/88/full), [DNR permit rules](https://www.dnr.state.mn.us/forestry/fire/questions.html), [August lift notice](https://www.dnr.state.mn.us/news/2026/08/24/elevated-burning-restrictions-are-reduced-northeastern-minnesota), [Superior National Forest alerts](https://www.fs.usda.gov/r09/superior/alerts).
- **Verification.** The local cron and delayed merge published one scoped record, and all 87 county endpoints agreed with the fixture counts. All 107 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Minnesota parsing took 1.470 ms p95. The existing 51-area projection took 10.743 ms p95. All Minnesota links passed. The sitewide check had 84 OK, nine blocked and one failure. Tennessee's burn-ban page reset the Bun connection on both checks and returned HTTP 200 in curl.

## MN county pass (2026-11-24)

- **Coverage.** Fixture replay at 10:55 p.m. Central moved 54 counties from unknown to restricted. The total is 55 restricted and 32 unknown, with zero active countywide bans and zero all-clears. The additions come from standing local rules. County rules apply to the named activities, and city, township and Tribal rules appear with geographic scope. [Fixture sources and timestamps](../src/worker/sources/mn/fixtures/README.md).
- **Live county orders.** Olmsted's sheriff archive is checked hourly, with a one-second pause between requests to the publisher. The adapter requires the sheriff's explicit countywide scope and matches the order with its dated lift. Open-ended reports have a 30-day review window. An empty or older archive leaves current countywide status unconfirmed. The archived 2023 ban was lifted at 8 a.m. Central on September 7. [Sheriff archive](https://www.olmstedcounty.gov/government/county-departments/sheriffs-office/sheriffs-office-news), [lift notice](https://www.olmstedcounty.gov/government/county-departments/sheriffs-office/sheriffs-office-news/burn-ban-olmsted-county-ended-800-am-thursday-september-7-2023).
- **Recurring rules.** 49 daily Central-time sources verify the standing rules against the selected HTML region, PDF hash or strong document ETag. They use the shared local calendar and positive-only mode. The new heading helper verifies an individual section within a municipal widget. It covers Blue Earth and Nicollet County. The HTTP helper recognizes an explicit Radware challenge even when it arrives as HTTP 200. Leech Lake and White Earth rules use the published reservation footprints and apply only within the authority's jurisdiction. [Leech Lake reservation assessment](https://www.llojibwe.org/drm/nps_assmt.pdf), [White Earth reservation map](https://www.whiteearth.com/media/pages/community/map-of-the-nation/d09e871560-1727389495/white-earth-base-map-24x36-w_allotments.pdf).
- **County feeds and permits.** The county sweep started with the metro and other high-population counties and checked all 87 county names, including sheriff, emergency management, fire, ordinance and board sources. Public ArcGIS searches for Minnesota burn bans and burning restrictions yielded one historical drought story map and zero restriction layers. The DNR planning dashboard and Google My Maps search yielded no separate current county order list. Chippewa Survey123, Mower GeoPermits and Moorhead's public form accept permit applications and expose no daily open or closed flag. Brown, Freeborn, Lake and Mower public Alert Centers had no active burn alert. The absence of an alert supplies no county status. [County directory](https://sos.mn.gov/about-minnesota/minnesota-government/county-government-links/), [ArcGIS burn ban search](https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/search?f=json&q=%22Minnesota%22%20AND%20%22burn%20ban%22&num=25), [DNR planning dashboard](https://www.dnr.state.mn.us/forestry/fire/planning.html), [Chippewa public form metadata](https://chippewa.maps.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/e81530cb3d6d420f8507a2341f2da44c?f=json), [Mower permit guidance](https://www.co.mower.mn.us/202/Environmental-Services), [Moorhead permit form](https://apps.cityofmoorhead.com/permits/openburn/).
- **Other alerts.** The DNR situation report last showed a November 13 edit, carries a brief county list with no restriction type, and directs readers to the existing map. Its area reports are forestry operations briefings with assorted update dates and geographic shorthand. The MPCA air-quality pages returned a Radware challenge. The NWS relay had no active Minnesota Air Quality Alerts in this capture. Several local ordinances have alert-triggered limits, so the MPCA alert boundary and effective-time feed remains a lead. No public current Nixle or Everbridge ban list surfaced. [DNR situation report](https://webapps.dnr.state.mn.us/fire_reporting_and_billing/cms/situation_report), [MPCA conditions](https://www.pca.state.mn.us/air-water-land-climate/current-air-quality-conditions), [NWS active relay](https://api.weather.gov/alerts/active?area=MN&event=Air%20Quality%20Alert).
- **Blocked and human-only.** Curl hit 403 on the Carver, Scott and Lyon permit pages and the Isanti and Rice county sites. Blue Earth's main host returned 403 for permit pages and its official CivicPlus alias served the content. The alternate fire and city sources used below have their own scope. The county directory's legacy URLs also had DNS and TLS failures. Pine provides public sign-up links for fire or emergency notifications. Dodge's sheriff links to its Facebook and X pages. Social links are for human use. [Carver permits](https://www.carvercountymn.gov/departments/public-services/environmental-services/disposal/open-burning), [Scott permits](https://www.scottcountymn.gov/1960/Open-Burning), [Lyon permits](https://www.lyoncountymn.gov/departments/sheriff/general-information/burn-permits), [Blue Earth primary](https://www.blueearthcountymn.gov/429/Permits), [Pine City alerts](https://portal.civicplus.com/MN-PineCity/notifications?tab=alerts), [Pine County alerts](https://member.everbridge.net/index/431700047822920#/signup), [Pine City Fire Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/Pine-City-Fire-Department-424488237610427/), [Dodge Sheriff Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/DCSOSheriff/), [Dodge Sheriff X](https://x.com/DCSOSheriff).
- **Verification.** All 49 rule documents parsed from curl responses and the current Olmsted archive yielded zero active orders. Moorhead and Rochester regenerate their accordion IDs between requests. Their readers select the published rule text. Bun passed 614 tests and TypeScript passed. Every new fixture stayed under 7 ms p95 thread CPU. The sitewide link check reported 671 OK, 37 access blocks and three failures across 711 URLs. All registered Minnesota links passed. Curl reproduced the Arkansas Forestry TLS chain error, the Joplin permit page timeout and the Tennessee permit page connection reset.

## 2026-11-24 Ohio (OH)

- **Restrictions.** Ohio's seasonal rule covers unincorporated areas outside railroad-controlled land in all 88 counties. On Nov. 24, the 6 a.m.–6 p.m. Eastern window gives 88 scoped restrictions, zero countywide bans and zero all-clears. Outside those hours, all 88 county statuses are unconfirmed. The same hours apply in March through May and October through November. The rule covers burning wood, brush, weeds, grass or rubbish and has a 200-foot exception for certain locations. [Ohio Revised Code § 1503.18](https://codes.ohio.gov/ohio-revised-code/section-1503.18), [State Fire Marshal guide](https://dam.assets.ohio.gov/image/upload/com.ohio.gov/documents/fire_ForestryLaws.pdf).
- **Statute / calendar and HTML.** One daily GET after Eastern midnight uses `lib/http.ts`, `lib/html.ts` and `lib/hash.ts` to verify the current text of clauses (C) and (D). The shared `calendarDayWindows` helper in `lib/calendar.ts` uses `lib/dates.ts` to set UTC validity from local dates and hours. A changed clause fails the poll for review. The source supplies scoped, positive-only reports for the day's window. [Fixture and request](../src/worker/sources/oh/fixtures/README.md).
- **Authority and orders.** The governor can further limit when and where fires are allowed by executive order. The forestry chief may grant waivers. The State Fire Marshal can issue emergency bans statewide or for part of the state, and authorized local fire officials can issue local bans. The September 20 and October 3, 2024 drought orders were rescinded October 11, 2024. No current executive order, emergency order or consolidated current list surfaced. Check the local fire department for the address and Ohio EPA for material and permit rules. [Forestry waivers](https://codes.ohio.gov/ohio-administrative-code/rule-1501%3A3-13-01), [Ohio Fire Code](https://codes.ohio.gov/ohio-administrative-code/rule-1301%3A7-7-03), [signed rescission](https://dam.assets.ohio.gov/image/upload/com.ohio.gov/SFM/SFM_-_Open_Burning_Order_Recission_10-11-2024.pdf), [Ohio EPA guidance](https://ohioepa.custhelp.com/app/answers/detail/a_id/2517/~/ohio-epas-regulations-on-open-burning).
- **Local pages and access.** Chardon's Oct. 1, 2026 notice relays the seasonal rule. Cuyahoga Falls and Franklin Township in Warren County publish standing fire and permit conditions. All three were reachable. [Chardon](https://www.cityofchardon.gov/m/newsflash/home/detail/293), [Cuyahoga Falls](https://www.cityofcf.com/departments/fire/open-burning), [Franklin Township](https://www.ftfd.org/fire-safety/open-burning).
- Direct requests to several state guidance pages returned HTTP 404. The statute, Fire Marshal PDF and EPA support article returned 200. [Fire Marshal guide page](https://com.ohio.gov/divisions-and-programs/state-fire-marshal/fire-prevention/fire-safety-guides/outdoor-burning-rules), [former ODNR rules page](https://ohiodnr.gov/discover-and-learn/safety-conservation/about-odnr/forestry/fire-management-program/open-burn-laws), [ODNR fall release](https://ohiodnr.gov/discover-and-learn/safety-conservation/about-ODNR/news/fall-wildfire-season-2026), [Fire Marshal October release](https://com.ohio.gov/about-us/media-center/news/bonfire-and-campfire-safety), [Ohio.gov summary](https://ohio.gov/residents/living-in-ohio/personal-and-family-safety/fire-prevention), [Ohio EPA portal](https://epa.ohio.gov/divisions-and-offices/air-pollution-control/open-burning).
- **Verification.** At 12:52 a.m. Eastern, the local cron and delayed merge published 88 upcoming records. All 88 county endpoints returned unknown with the upcoming window, and the active list was empty. The fixture merge produced 88 scoped restrictions at 6 a.m. and expired them at 6 p.m. All 112 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Ohio parsed in 0.341 ms p95, current sources merged and encoded in 5.472 ms p95, and the existing 51-area projection took 10.761 ms p95. The final link check reported 85 OK, 11 blocked and zero failed. All Ohio links in the checker passed.
- **Rollout check.** The morning change from an upcoming rule to an active restriction is eligible for an alert under the current transition rules. Check whether subscribers should receive a recurring notice for their county each morning of the season.

## 2026-11-24 Georgia (GA)

- **Restrictions.** Georgia EPD's October–April rule covers 27 counties and generally limits burning storm debris, burning for weed, disease or pest control, and burning for land clearing, construction or right-of-way maintenance. The May–September rule covers 54 counties, including 19 with limits on prescribed burning. EPD's rule index, maps, April 29 announcement and one-page guide agree; the Forestry Commission's September notice confirms the summer rule ended October 1. The statewide garbage-burning prohibition and hand-piled debris safety requirements also apply. [EPD rules](https://epd.georgia.gov/air-protection-branch/open-burning-rules-georgia), [Rule 391-3-1-.02(5)](https://rules.sos.ga.gov/GAC/391-3-1), [permit and safety rules](https://gatrees.org/burn-permits-and-notifications/).
- **Gwinnett.** At 12:56 a.m. Eastern the county still showed November 23 as a burn day. The Fire Marshal publishes by 9 a.m. The decision covers the Fire and EMS District, which excludes the City of Loganville. The county's annual notice is historical, and the Langley View medical zone PDF expired September 30, 2026. The medical map's two ArcGIS layers returned HTTP 200 with application error 403. Check the county's daily decision, the medical map and the local fire authority for an address, or call 678-518-4979. [Daily bulletin](https://www.gwinnettcounty.com/government/departments/fire-emergency/outdoor-burning-information), [fire district boundaries](https://www.gwinnettcounty.com/government/departments/county-administrator/assessor/service-districts), [medical-zone viewer](https://experience.arcgis.com/experience/d8fd2e58d76442d0b79372a2d639bfba), [medical area layer](https://utility.arcgis.com/usrsvcs/servers/7c377a0102774055a790fdc021f72fab/rest/services/Burn_Ban_Buffer_Areas_view/FeatureServer/3?f=json), [medical point layer](https://utility.arcgis.com/usrsvcs/servers/a5ee96bddfac4cb5a198c986bb6cdfaa/rest/services/Burn_Ban_Intersection_Points_view/FeatureServer/4?f=json).
- **Emergency orders.** The State Forester can issue orders for counties or parts of counties, and local fire officials can add local restrictions. The April emergency order covered 91 counties and was reduced to 17. The May map is historical, and the governor's June order says the wildfire emergency expired May 22. No current statewide emergency order surfaced. GFC's fiResponse map remains manual. Its public daily summary returned 130 area entries without order types or dates, and a historical query for the known May ban returned an empty array. The restriction-type catalog returned HTTP 401 and the separate ArcGIS EPD geography layer returned HTTP 403. Use the GFC map, county GFC office or local fire marshal to verify current orders and agricultural or land-clearing permit availability. [GFC map](https://georgiafc.firesponse.com/public/), [revised order notice](https://gatrees.org/southern-georgia-burning-restrictions-eased/), [June executive order](https://gov.georgia.gov/document/2026-executive-order/06122601/download), [county offices](https://gatrees.org/about/county-contacts/), [restriction-type catalog](https://georgiafc.firesponse.com/burn-permit/api/restrictiontype), [EPD geography layer](https://utility.arcgis.com/usrsvcs/servers/25723eb546574ea1b0e03a217ab4f177/rest/services/Counties/GA_Counties_159/FeatureServer/0).
- **Fetchers.** The Liferay bulletin uses `lib/http.ts`, `lib/html.ts` and `lib/dates.ts` at 9 a.m., noon and 4 p.m. Eastern. A burn-day advisory leaves countywide status unconfirmed. The Drupal EPD lists use the same HTTP and HTML helpers plus `lib/calendar.ts`. The in-season rule polls after Eastern midnight, and both calendar sources are marked recurring. The new shared local-day window handles Eastern midnight across daylight saving changes. [Captured HTML and request URLs](../src/worker/sources/ga/fixtures/README.md).
- **Verification.** The local cron and delayed merge published 27 restricted counties, including Gwinnett and DeKalb. All 159 county endpoints agreed: 27 restricted, 132 unknown, zero countywide bans and zero all-clears. The Gwinnett source had no November 24 decision during the morning check. All 119 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Fixture p95 times were 0.157 ms for Gwinnett, 0.482 ms for the summer rule and 0.342 ms for the October–April rule. The existing 51-area projection still crossed the review threshold at 10.879 ms p95. All registered Georgia links passed. The sitewide retry had 93 OK, nine blocked and one failure, a Tennessee burn-ban page connection reset. The initial Mississippi feed timeout cleared on retry.

## GA county pass (2026-11-24)

- **Result.** The November 24 live registry fetches and merge added 22 scoped restrictions to the 27 counties in the existing October–April snapshot. All 159 counties resolved to 49 restricted and 110 unknown. No countywide bans or all-clears were verified. The 27 new recurring sources describe standing local requirements. Four positive-only fetchers cover the Barrow, Habersham and Ware permit calendars and Hinesville's live residential permit form. The three calendars had no November 24 closure entries. Hinesville's linked form reported a closure for city residents in Liberty County.
- **Permit systems and maps.** Georgia Forestry's newer permit application has location-based decisions for covered activities. Its restrictions request returned HTTP 401 and the application requires sign-in. The public ArcGIS search returned the same EPD geography service recorded in the statewide pass. GFC's public catalog also exposes wildfire incidents and prescribed-fire activity, which carry no current county order status. Searches for KML, public Nixle and Everbridge notices and county alert feeds found no additional machine-readable county decisions. [GFC permit system](https://gatrees.org/fire-prevention-suppression/online-mobile-burn-permit-system/), [EPD ArcGIS item](https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=25723eb546574ea1b0e03a217ab4f177), [GFC county contacts](https://gatrees.org/gfc-contacts-2/county-contacts/).
- **Local publishers.** The separate Georgia Burn Permits portal uses Firestore and listed Barrow, Habersham and Ware as enabled. Each county links to that portal. The fetchers verify enrollment and read only date and closure-reason fields from the local-day calendar. The Hinesville source follows the form currently selected by the city and reads the closure text in its public instructions. The standing rules validate publisher HTML or document bytes and use the Eastern calendar helper. Residential permits, municipal prohibitions, hours and land-clearing limits preserve the issuing authority's geographic scope. [Portal](https://app.georgiaburnpermits.com/), [Hinesville permits](https://www.cityofhinesville.org/123/Burn-Permits), [exact requests and fixtures](../src/worker/sources/ga/fixtures/README.md).
- **Access blocks.** The GFC permit API returned 401. Municode denied direct reads for Athens-Clarke, Augusta-Richmond, Banks, Bleckley, Effingham, Hart and Sumter code pages. Official county copies and fire-department guidance supplied alternatives in several counties. Bryan and Catoosa's Granicus sites and Cordele's current site returned 403, as did Gainesville's CivicPlus permit page. Elbert Fire's site failed TLS hostname verification. Heard's ordinance, Glynn's legacy ordinance URL and several archived documents returned 404. Lowndes's legacy lift-notice host failed DNS. Social links are for human verification.
- **Verification.** Live calls through the 31 new registered sources all parsed successfully. All 459 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Each GA fixture stayed under 4 ms p95 thread CPU. The benchmark measured 0.091 ms p95 for the Firestore query result, 0.169 ms for the Hinesville page and form, and up to 2.899 ms for the reviewed local rule fixtures. The 51-area projection remained above its review threshold at 10.922 ms p95. The link check reported 414 OK, 28 access blocks and four failures across 446 URLs. All GA links in the checker passed. The Arkansas Forestry portal failed certificate-chain verification. Tennessee's burn-ban and permit pages reset connections. A direct retry gave 403 for the burn-ban page and another reset for the permit page. Washington's Northwest Clean Air Agency timed out and returned 200 on direct retry.

The search covered all 159 county names. Targeted county-seat and fire-agency searches followed where local results were sparse. “Nothing found” means the search surfaced no separate local status or actionable rule. County contacts and state guidance can still help in those areas.

Human-only social channels linked from local publishers include [Bulloch County on Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/bullochboc), [Clayton Fire & EMS on Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/ClaytonCountyFireEmergencyServices), [Glynn County on Nextdoor](https://nextdoor.com/agency-detail/ga/glynn/glynn-county-board-of-commissioners/), [Heard EMA on X](https://twitter.com/heardcountyema), [Ware EMA on Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/warecountyema) and [White County on Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/WhiteCoGov).

The emergency RSS feeds for Camden, Lowndes and Hinesville each returned HTTP 200 with zero items. [Camden EMA](https://www.camdencountyga.gov/RSSFeed.aspx?ModID=63&CID=Emergency-Management-Agency-EMA-7), [Lowndes emergency alerts](https://www.lowndescounty.com/RSSFeed.aspx?ModID=63&CID=Emergency-Alerts-5), [Hinesville emergency notifications](https://www.cityofhinesville.org/RSSFeed.aspx?ModID=63&CID=Emergency-Notifications-2).

## 2026-11-24 Arizona (AZ)

- **Restrictions.** Coconino has two scoped National Park Service reports, South Rim Stage 1 and North Rim Stage 2. The November 4 bulletin and rescission agree. Mohave remains unconfirmed because the park crosses the county line and the named rim orders lack a verified Mohave boundary. Pima's undated current widget reports no restrictions on county-owned property and leaves countywide status unconfirmed. The Board delegated implementation and rescission to the county administrator under Resolution 2026-34. Across 15 counties, one is restricted, 14 are unknown, and zero have countywide bans or all-clears. The captures were late November 23 Arizona time, on November 24 UTC. [NPS bulletin](https://www.nps.gov/grca/learn/management/fireinformation.htm), [November 4 notice](https://www.nps.gov/grca/learn/news/stage-2-fire-restrictions-rescinded-on-south-rim.htm), [Pima widget](https://www.pima.gov/wildfire-warnings), [resolution](https://pima.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?GUID=5F1F153D-A262-416B-AFA2-E97FF5767366&ID=8074876&Options=&Search=).
- **Fetchers.** Pima uses a new `lib/civicplus.ts` widget helper with `lib/html.ts` and `lib/http.ts`. The NPS CommonSpot bulletin uses the HTML, HTTP and date helpers. Its reports expire 30 days after the displayed update if NPS leaves that date unchanged. The Arizona Legislature HTML and `lib/calendar.ts` verify § 49-501 and apply the recurring May 1–September 30 restriction in Area A portions of Maricopa, Pinal and Yavapai. The rule is out of season and its scheduled changes suppress alerts. [Fixtures and request URLs](../src/worker/sources/az/fixtures/README.md), [Area A boundaries](https://www.azleg.gov/ars/49/00541.htm).
- **Interagency map.** The Dashboard, Experience and WebMap JSON all responded. The county layer has blank stages in all 15 Arizona rows. DFFM's Gila Stage 2 and Coconino Northwest Stage 1 contradict the July rescission. The BLM Arizona Strip layer retains Stage 1 after the September 8 lift. Reclamation's order expired when all BLM offices lifted their seasonal restrictions. The BIA Navajo row has a blank stage and a September date beside older Stage 1 wording. Check the land manager's order and later rescissions for the location, especially Navajo Nation. [Southwest web-map layers](https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/3cc937c423e44a678da007a70a8f8598/data?f=json), [DFFM rescission](https://dffm.az.gov/news/arizona-department-forestry-and-fire-management-lifting-fire-restrictions), [BLM district status](https://www.blm.gov/programs/public-safety-and-fire/fire/regional-info/arizona/fire-restrictions), [Navajo Nation orders](https://opvp.navajo-nsn.gov/executive-orders/).
- **Local rules and access.** State foresters, local governments and land managers issue restrictions for their jurisdictions. Maricopa, Pinal and Pima issue their own air-quality permits. Maricopa's Burning Activities and Outreach pages describe standing rules and No Burn Day thresholds. The linked advisory returned 404. Use the county's Clean Air app or 602-506-6400 for the declaration. Direct GET returned 403 for the DFFM restrictions hub, the Coconino County July-stage page and lift notice, and the May Prescott/CAFMA PDF. Prescott/CAFMA lifted Stage 1 July 21 and Mohave announced its final unincorporated-area lift October 6. [State forester authority](https://www.azleg.gov/ars/37/01303.htm), [local emergency authority](https://www.azleg.gov/ars/26/00311.htm), [ADEQ permits](https://azdeq.gov/OpenBurn), [Maricopa activities](https://www.maricopa.gov/1830/Burning-Activities), [CAFMA notices](https://www.cazfire.gov/369/Wildland-Fires), [Mohave notice](https://www.mohave.gov/news-notices/posts/county-outdoor-fire-and-fireworks-prohibition-lifted-10626/).
- **Verification.** The local cron and delayed merge published both named rim orders, and the coverage and all 15 county endpoints agreed with the fixture results. All 127 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Fixture p95 was 0.107 ms for Pima, 0.289 ms for Grand Canyon and 0.401 ms for Area A. Full Pima HTML parsed in 3.859 ms p95. The existing 51-area projection exceeded the 7 ms review threshold at 9.807 ms p95. The link check reported 99 OK, 11 blocked and zero failures. DFFM, ADEQ and Coconino County were the three blocked Arizona links in the checker.

## 2026-11-24 Washington (WA)

- **Restrictions.** Ecology lists Stage 1 in the Methow Valley (Okanogan County) from November 18 at 9 a.m. Pacific and reports the Northwest Clean Air Agency's Stage 2 in the Columbia Valley urban growth area (Whatcom County) from 10 a.m. Pacific. Both are until further notice. The Puget Sound grid shows no current air-quality ban across seven areas of King, Kitsap, Pierce and Snohomish. Unincorporated King's fire-safety page is clear since the October 1 lift. The result is six counties with scoped reports, two restricted and 37 unknown statewide, with zero countywide bans or all-clears. [Ecology](https://ecology.wa.gov/air-climate/air-quality/smoke-fire/burn-bans), [Northwest Clean Air Agency order](https://nwcleanairwa.gov/news-release/nwcaa-issues-a-stage-2-air-quality-burn-ban-for-whatcom-countys-columbia-valley-urban-growth-area/), [Puget Sound status and map](https://pscleanair.gov/168/Air-Quality-Burn-Ban-Status), [King County rescission](https://kingcountylocalblog.com/2026/09/30/with-cooler-wetter-conditions-king-county-lifts-burn-ban/).
- **Fetchers.** Kentico/ASP.NET stage tables poll every four hours, the CivicPlus portal's IIS grid polls hourly, and King County's Sitecore current-status block polls three times daily. They use `lib/http.ts` and `lib/html.ts`. The HTML helper can associate tables with paragraph labels and extract CSS-selected grids. `lib/dates.ts` now reads whole-hour times with a.m./p.m. All reports use partial scope and positive-only mode. Ecology's source maps verified subareas and county names. Add a mapping if Ecology lists a new local area. [Captured markup and requests](../src/worker/sources/wa/fixtures/README.md).
- **DNR and statewide authority.** The 23-row ArcGIS layer returned 21 code 99 rows and two code 4 rows. The Olympics row refers to federal land managers. The Valley FDRA note repeats the Methow Valley order, while the separately named Methow FDRA carries October guidance. Fire-danger polygons and free-text exceptions do not establish the geography of an individual order, and the layer exposes no data edit stamp. Use the DNR portal for protected lands. The governor's emergency outdoor ban ended September 30. Ecology handles air-quality bans in counties without a local clean air agency. Fire marshals, fire districts, Tribes and federal land managers may have additional orders. Standing urban-area and permit rules still apply. [DNR layer](https://gis.dnr.wa.gov/site3/rest/services/Public_Wildfire/WADNR_PUBLIC_WD_WildfireDanger/MapServer/1), [DNR burn restrictions](https://dnr.wa.gov/wildfire-resources/outdoor-burning/burn-restrictions), [governor's order announcement](https://governor.wa.gov/news/2026/governor-ferguson-declares-statewide-wildfire-emergency-issues-statewide-burn-ban), [Ecology permits](https://ecology.wa.gov/regulations-permits/permits-certifications/air-quality-permits/burn-permits).
- **Local sources and access.** Clark lifted its debris and recreational bans October 1 and retains permit and area rules. Pierce and Snohomish current pages show no fire-safety ban, superseding their September Stage 1 notices. Spokane Clean Air's current page shows no temporary restriction and its outdoor-burning guide describes standing rules. District 2 still shows its June 30 ban message and District 9 points readers to county status. Everett alternated between a Cloudflare 403 and a guidance page distinguishing fire-safety and air-quality bans. South County Fire returned an Akamai 403. [Clark](https://clark.wa.gov/community-development/outdoor-burning), [Pierce](https://www.piercecountywa.gov/fire), [Snohomish](https://www.snohomishcountywa.gov/1144/Outdoor-Burning-Information), [Spokane](https://spokanecleanair.org/burning/burn-restrictions/), [District 2](https://www.scfd2.org/outdoor-burning/), [District 9](https://www.scfd9.org/can-i-burn), [Everett](https://www.everettwa.gov/2332/Outdoor-Burning), [South County Fire](https://www.southsnofire.org/about-us/divisions/fire-marshal/burn-bans-and-regulations).
- **Verification.** The main King County hostname failed the local Worker TLS handshake. The county's public content host served the identical current block and worked in the Worker. The hourly cron and delayed merge published all three sources, and all 39 county endpoints agreed with the fixture counts. The 139 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Fixture p95 was 0.315 ms for Ecology, 0.132 ms for Puget Sound and 0.081 ms for King County. The existing 51-area projection was 10.452 ms p95. The final link check had 106 OK, 14 access blocks and zero failures. [King County content host](https://cd.kingcounty.gov/en/dept/local-services/governance-leadership/local-government-for-unincorporated-king-county/fire-safety).

## 2026-11-24 Colorado (CO)

- **Restrictions.** Nine counties had scoped reports. Adams, Arapahoe, Boulder, Broomfield, Denver, Douglas and Jefferson had CDPHE indoor wood-burning limits through 4 p.m. Mountain time November 24 in AIR program areas at or below 7,000 feet, with appliance and municipal exceptions. The Estes Valley fire chief's Stage 1 outdoor order applies within the district in Larimer County from September 4 at 8 a.m. until changed or lifted. Across 64 counties, eight were restricted, 56 unknown, zero countywide bans and zero all-clears. [CDPHE advisory](https://www.colorado.gov/airquality/advisory.aspx), [Estes Valley current status](https://www.estesvalleyfire.org/fire-restrictions-bans), [dated captures and request URLs](../src/worker/sources/co/fixtures/README.md).
- **County reports.** Douglas's September 17 lift covered unincorporated areas, Larkspur and Castle Pines. The Douglas methodology describes jurisdiction and decision criteria. El Paso's current page corroborates its September 30 lift. Boulder enacted western Stage 2 and eastern Stage 1 June 30, rescinded eastern Stage 1 and reduced the west to Stage 1 September 9, and lifted western Stage 1 October 15. Its current page shows no seasonal order. Ordinance 2023-1 has separate weather-triggered limits. Arapahoe's maintained table shows a September 21 lift. The June Stage 2 incident page is historical and the Stage 2 sign PDF now returns 404. Sable Altura's dated Stage 1 text names its Arapahoe area and appears with older no-ban artwork, so verify with the district. Aegis shows no unincorporated Larimer order at any elevation after the September extension expired November 3. [Douglas methodology](https://dcsheriff.net/documents/douglas-county-fire-restriction-criteria-and-methodology.pdf/), [El Paso](https://epcsheriffsoffice.com/services/fire-information/), [Boulder September notice](https://content.govdelivery.com/accounts/COBOULDER/bulletins/429502a), [October notice](https://content.govdelivery.com/accounts/COBOULDER/bulletins/42f51aa), [Arapahoe](https://www.arapahoeco.gov/burnbaninformation), [Sable Altura](https://sablealturafire.org/fireprevention/), [Larimer Aegis](https://aegis.larimer.gov/pages/fire-danger).
- **Rules and access.** County commissioners and sheriffs issue county orders under local authority. Municipalities, fire districts and federal land managers set rules for their own jurisdictions. CSFS links to the DFPC directory, which returned CloudFront 403. Both regional ArcGIS item requests succeeded. The map has federal and tribal layers and Wyoming county orders. Its Colorado county boundaries have no restriction fields. BLM field offices list seasonal lifts and standing rules, with a separate Gunnison-area access closure. Jefferson Public Health describes permits for agricultural and forestry burns from 6,400 feet. The open-burning forecast reports a metro winter closure through February and limited permitted exceptions. Use the local issuer, land manager, and the open-burning forecast for the address and activity. The Larimer September notice returned Cloudflare 403. [CSFS directory](https://csfs.colostate.edu/wildfire-mitigation/current-wildfire-information-fire-restrictions/), [regional dashboard](https://www.arcgis.com/apps/dashboards/407bde860acc492bb53c972275bfbd33), [BLM](https://www.blm.gov/programs/fire/regional-info/colorado/fire-restrictions), [Jefferson permits](https://www.jeffco.us/2356/Open-Burning), [CDPHE open-burning forecast](https://www.colorado.gov/airquality/burn_forecast.aspx).
- **Fetchers.** The Douglas and El Paso REST pages use the shared public WordPress page validator and the HTML extractor. Boulder, Arapahoe and Estes Valley use rendered WordPress, Revize and Streamline HTML. Larimer reads the public ArcGIS Experience JSON and checks that all three elevation widgets agree with the county overview. These sources poll three times daily Mountain time. The CDPHE ASP.NET bulletin polls hourly, with publisher times interpreted by `lib/dates.ts`. All use `lib/http.ts` and positive-only, partial scope. A clear county page leaves countywide status unknown.
- **Verification.** The local midnight Mountain cron published all seven sources after the two-minute merge. The coverage, active restrictions and all 64 county endpoints agreed with the counts above. All 152 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Colorado fixture p95 ranged from 0.075 to 1.022 ms and full publisher responses stayed under 3 ms p95 in Bun. The final current-source merge and synthetic 51-area projection crossed the 7 ms review threshold at 7.410 and 14.641 ms p95. The final link check had 122 OK, 15 blocked and one failure, an existing Tennessee connection reset. The Hawaii timeout from the first run cleared on retry. The DFPC directory was the only blocked Colorado link in the checker.

## CO county pass (2026-11-24)

The county pass checked all 64 counties against the November 24 baseline of eight restricted and 56 unknown. Replaying the new captures moves 40 previously unknown counties to **restricted**. That includes five with current local orders and 35 with standing rules as their current evidence. Cheyenne’s open-burn ban applies to unincorporated areas, so its county summary is restricted and its local order is a ban. The other four current orders are Stage 1 in Elbert, Fremont, Grand Fire Protection District No. 1, and the Upper Piedra part of Hinsdale. The baseline plus the new reports gives 48 restricted and 16 unknown while the baseline advisories are still valid, with zero countywide bans or all-clears. The new capture window was November 25 Mountain time. This comparison measures coverage against the original baseline and the replay preserves the sources' publication dates and validity.

The registry adds official status panels and notices in CivicPlus, Drupal, WordPress, Revize, Streamline and plain HTML, plus a sheriff JSON API and a Google My Maps KML map. All use positive-only mode. Standing local permit, notification and seasonal rules use the reviewed HTML or PDF calendar helper with `recurring: true`. The HTML helper rejects multiple matching decisions, the PDF helper checks strong document validators, and the KML helper resolves published outline colors. [Request URLs and capture details](../src/worker/sources/co/fixtures/county-pass/README.md)

The state directory still returns CloudFront 403 and the Rocky Mountain dashboard’s Colorado county layer still contains boundaries. The Eagle County OEM’s older state map link returned a TLS EOF. West Slope Fire Info provides a regional collection of county pages, with differing update histories. Its Mesa panel retains an earlier Stage 1 after the sheriff’s September 18 lift. Park’s district FeatureServer has mixed `BAN` and danger values, July 2025 edit metadata and empty verification dates. Montrose’s county MapServer agrees with the regional clear panel, with no last-edit timestamp on the restriction layer. The private Colorado Fire Ban and BurnBound maps were used as leads to publishers. [DFPC directory](https://dfpc.colorado.gov/firerestriction), [DHSEM legacy link](https://www.coemergency.com/p/fire-bans-danger.html), [West Slope Fire Info](https://westslopefireinfo.com/), [Park layer](https://services3.arcgis.com/atP7uTVHA2caR3p6/arcgis/rest/services/Fire_Ban_Map_WFL1/FeatureServer/5), [Montrose layer](https://mcmap.montrosecounty.net/server/rest/services/fire/MontroseCOfirerestrictions/MapServer/36).

**Verification.** All 25 status sources and 35 rule sources returned HTTP 200 in direct curl checks and parsed successfully from the full responses. All 711 tests passed with `bun test --retry=4`, and TypeScript passed. The slowest new fixture took 3.134 ms p95 CPU time in that run. Existing wall-clock timing failures also reproduced on the untouched baseline under host load. The link checker recorded 1,072 OK, 39 blocked and five failures. The previously known DFPC block was the only Colorado result in those last two groups. A curl retry confirmed two Laketown Township HTTP 429s and the Arkansas Forestry certificate-chain error. Both Sacramento URLs returned HTTP 200 on retry after connection resets in the checker.

## 2026-11-24 Oregon (OR)

- **Restrictions.** For November 24 Pacific time, Lane County is restricted and 35 counties are unknown. There are zero countywide bans and zero countywide all-clears. LRAPA prohibits residential woody yard-debris burning in Eugene city limits, Eugene's urban growth area outside the city, Florence city limits, Oakridge and its urban growth boundary, and Springfield and its urban growth boundary. Its coastal and elsewhere areas have a 10 a.m.–4 p.m. Pacific window where local rules allow burning. Both home-heating areas were green through 4 p.m. Pacific with their regular smoke-opacity limits. [LRAPA outdoor advisory](https://www.lrapa-or.gov/air-quality-protection/burning-overview/outdoor-burning/#current-restrictions), [wood heating](https://www.lrapa-or.gov/air-quality-protection/burning-overview/wood-heating/), [Title 47](https://www.lrapa-or.gov/download/35/current-rules/9405/title-47-outdoor-burning).
- **Wildland restrictions.** ODF ended the 2026 restrictions across its districts on November 4. The June West Oregon proclamation and Jackson County's October 26 low-danger bulletin with closed debris burning predate that lift. The ArcGIS edit view returned application error 499 and its HTML redirected to sign-in. The separate public view used by the map returned 61 regulated-use areas with zero active debris-burning or campfire codes. The adapter maps verified area overlaps in 35 counties and reports positive restrictions on ODF-protected lands. The State Forester's orders apply to the lands they name. Check the ODF map and district proclamation for an address, and the relevant federal or tribal land manager on its lands. [ODF lift](https://content.govdelivery.com/accounts/ORFORESTRY/bulletins/431ad22), [West Oregon proclamation](https://www.oregon.gov/odf/fire/Documents/RUWO.pdf), [Jackson County bulletin](https://readyjacksonco.com/Get-Ready/Know-Your-Hazards/fire-danger-levels), [ODF map](https://gisapps.odf.oregon.gov/firerestrictions/pfr.html), [ORS 477](https://www.oregonlegislature.gov/bills_laws/ors/ors477.html), [BLM orders](https://www.blm.gov/programs/fire/regional-info/oregon-washington/fire-restrictions).
- **Willamette Valley.** At verification the DEQ and ODF bulletin had published through November 23. That day's backyard prohibition covered special control areas within Zone 604 in parts of Benton, Clackamas, Linn, Marion, Multnomah, Polk, Washington and Yamhill. Its agricultural text advised against burning. The adapter reads the applicable backyard decision and expires it at Pacific midnight. Friday and holiday bulletins can list separate dates and hours. Check the new day's bulletin, the DEQ address map and the local fire district. [Daily bulletin](https://smkmgt.com/burn.php), [control-area rule](https://secure.sos.state.or.us/oard/viewSingleRule.action?ruleVrsnRsn=256251), [DEQ open burning](https://www.oregon.gov/deq/aq/pages/burning.aspx), [ODA activity guidance](https://www.oregon.gov/oda/agriculture-services/smoke-burning/pages/agricultural-backyard-burning.aspx).
- **Local checks.** Marion County's SharePoint page describes permits and points to district phone lines. Clackamas Fire's WordPress burn status rendered a shortcode placeholder, and its outdoor rules direct callers to 503-742-2945. TVF&R's two CivicPlus pages give seasonal rules and a phone line. Its dated notice confirms Washington County fire agencies lifted the summer high-danger ban October 15. Both Hillsboro Granicus pages returned HTTP 403. The city's published rules describe a standing open-burning ordinance. Junction City requires permits and uses LRAPA's daily decision. Lane Fire Authority says its yard-debris season opened October 17 and also directs readers to LRAPA. Local fire agencies can impose additional permit and fire-safety limits. [Marion](https://co.marion.or.us/PW/Planning/Pages/Backyard%20Burn%20Permits.aspx), [Clackamas](https://clackamasfire.com/outdoor-burning-notice/), [TVF&R rules](https://www.tvfr.com/356/Outdoor-Burning), [Washington County lift](https://www.tvfr.gov/m/newsflash/home/detail/843), [Junction City](https://www.jcfire.org/outdoor-burning), [Lane Fire Authority](https://www.lanefire.org/fire-and-burn-season-information), [ORS 478](https://www.oregonlegislature.gov/bills_laws/ors/ors478.html).
- **Fetchers.** LRAPA's ASP.NET MVC app publishes its advisory records in small Syncfusion grids. Both sources use the new `lib/aspnet.ts` Syncfusion JSON helper and the existing HTML, date and HTTP helpers. The PHP Willamette bulletin uses HTML, date and HTTP helpers. ODF uses `lib/arcgis.ts` for complete attribute reads, coded domains and edit freshness. All four are scoped, positive-only publisher reports. [Captures, request URLs and ODF county mapping](../src/worker/sources/or/fixtures/README.md).
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge checked the four feeds successfully. Coverage, the restrictions endpoint and all 36 county endpoints showed eight restricted counties from the November 23 Willamette bulletin, 28 unknown counties and seven upcoming Lane orders before Pacific midnight. The fixture merge at midnight verified the November 24 counts above. All 164 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Oregon fixture p95 times were 0.421 ms for outdoor LRAPA, 0.157 ms for wood heating, 0.147 ms for Willamette and 0.212 ms for ODF. The synthetic 51-area projection was 11.875 ms p95, above the repository's 7 ms review threshold. The final link check had 132 OK, 16 access blocks and one failure, a connection reset on the existing Tennessee page. Hillsboro was the only Oregon HTTP access block in that check.

## 2026-11-24 Kentucky (KY)

- **Restrictions.** Forestry reports county bans in Edmonson and Grayson. Edmonson's November 20 order excepts established campground campfires. Grayson's November 18 county order was quoted by local news. The 07:39 UTC snapshot had two bans, 118 unknown, zero active scoped restrictions and zero all-clears. During both zones' 6 a.m.–6 p.m. local windows, the same inputs give two bans and 118 counties with a scoped restriction. [Forestry current list](https://eec.ky.gov/Natural-Resources/Forestry/wildland-fire-management/pages/county-burn-bans.aspx), [Edmonson order](https://www.edmonsonvoice.com/uploads/3/7/6/7/37671151/screenshot-2026-11-20-154148_orig.png), [Grayson order coverage](https://www.k105.com/2026/11/19/grayson-co-under-an-open-burning-burn-ban/).
- **SharePoint.** The rendered page and public Pages REST field agree. REST supplies an editorial timestamp of November 20 at 23:41 UTC. The new `lib/sharepoint.ts` page validator and existing HTML and HTTP helpers read the list four times daily in positive-only mode. Published positives age out 30 days after the editorial timestamp, and outages retain the last good report for up to 24 hours. The archived empty wording clears the reported rows. Grayson's bare label is verified for this edition only because Grayson is also a city in Carter County. Recheck ambiguous names or a return to the older table layout with the publisher. [Publisher captures and exact requests](../src/worker/sources/ky/fixtures/README.md).
- **Authority and calendar.** Cities and counties can adopt emergency open-burning bans under KRS 149.401, and county judge-executives can implement county orders. Forestry's list depends on local reporting. KRS 149.400 limits daytime burning in or within 150 feet of woods or brush from February 15 through April 30 and October 1 through December 15. Snow cover and specified activities have exceptions. The daily recurring adapter verifies the statute with the shared PDF hash helper and uses `lib/calendar.ts` for 79 Eastern and 41 Central counties. Its scheduled windows suppress alerts. Check the county judge-executive, fire department or land manager for the location and activity. [Local authority](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=1899), [Forestry rules](https://eec.ky.gov/natural-resources/forestry/wildland-fire-management/pages/outdoor-burning-laws.aspx), [county offices](https://dlg.ky.gov/counties/Pages/county-list.aspx).
- **Local and air rules.** Franklin's WordPress article and Pendleton's SharePoint page describe the seasonal law and local checks. The state air-quality guide and allowable-burning PDF describe material restrictions, ozone-season limits in listed areas and Jefferson's year-round local rules. Louisville Metro's burn-plan guidance returned a Cloudflare 403 and remains manual. [Franklin](https://franklincounty.ky.gov/spring-burn-season-begins/), [Pendleton](https://pendletoncounty.ky.gov/Pages/Open-Burning-Laws.aspx), [Division for Air Quality](https://eec.ky.gov/Environmental-Protection/Air/Pages/Open-Burning.aspx), [Louisville Metro](https://louisvilleky.gov/government/air-pollution-control-district/open-burning-louisville).
- **Verification.** The hourly local cron and two-minute merge populated coverage, the active-bans feed and all 120 county endpoints. All 173 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Kentucky fixture p95 was 0.160 ms for SharePoint and 0.210 ms for the statute and calendar. The current-source merge measured 8.076 ms p95 and the synthetic 51-area run measured 11.322 ms, both above the 7 ms review threshold. The final link check had 138 OK, 17 blocked and zero failed. Louisville was the only Kentucky access block. The initial Lane County wood-heating timeout cleared on retry.

## 2026-11-24 National Weather Service fire weather

- **Feed and coverage.** The hourly queue polls the filtered NWS active alert feed. The live capture at 07:42 UTC contained no Red Flag Warnings or Fire Weather Watches. Three Special Weather Statements described fire danger and mapped to 20 county rows. The historical capture contains 27 real Red Flag Warning messages. The 2026-04-16 GIS build maps 3,536 fire-weather zones and 4,012 public forecast zones across the site's 3,143 counties. Fire zone MTZ133 crosses the Montana–South Dakota border. Kalawao County uses its Census land boundary because NWS codes it with Maui. [Fixture provenance](../src/worker/sources/nws/fixtures/README.md), [NWS fire weather zones](https://www.weather.gov/gis/firezones), [NWS forecast zone correlation](https://www.weather.gov/gis/ZoneCounty).
- **Legal triggers.** New Mexico prohibits starting a prescribed burn in the warned area under NMSA 1978 §68-5-3(C). Colorado rules cover unincorporated Baca, Boulder, Jefferson and Pueblo counties and the activities in Custer County's Stage 2 rules. Jefferson's Stage 1 restrictions extend through local midnight at the end of the last warned day. South Dakota's McPherson and Miner ordinances cover open fires outside municipalities and Miner's ordinance also covers burn barrels. [New Mexico enacted law](https://www.nmlegis.gov/Sessions/23%20Regular/final/SB0021.PDF), [Baca Ordinance No. 7](https://www.bacacountyco.gov/ordinance-no-7/), [Boulder Ordinance 2023-1](https://assets.bouldercounty.gov/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/ordinance-2023-1_fire_bans_open_burning.pdf), [Custer Ordinance 20-01](https://custercounty-co.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Custer-County-Colorado-Ordinance-20-01.pdf#page=6), [Jefferson standing order](https://www.jeffcosheriffco.gov/safety/wildland-fire/fire-restrictions/guide), [Pueblo Code §8.24.070](https://www.pueblocounty.gov/county-attorney/chapter-824-open-fire-and-open-burning-restriction), [McPherson Ordinance 21-1](https://mcpherson.sdcounty.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/01/Ordinance-21-1-Signed_Open-Burning-Regulation.pdf), [Miner Ordinance 2024-04](https://miner.sdcounty.gov/firedanger/).
- **North Dakota declarations.** The current Cass, McLean and Mercer county declarations explicitly use Red Flag Warnings. A matching warning prompts a single North Dakota DES query to verify the applicable declaration filename, active status and rescission flag. Cass's declaration runs through November 30. McLean and Mercer run through December 31. The source reports only the warning-triggered conditions from these declarations. [Cass declaration](https://www.casscountynd.gov/departments/emergency-management/cass-county-burn-restrictions), [McLean declaration](https://www.mcleancountynd.gov/county-news/2026-burn-restrictions/), [Mercer declaration](https://mercercountynd.com/image/cache/5-6-2026_Fire_Emergency_Declaration_Burn_Ban.pdf), [North Dakota DES guidance](https://www.des.nd.gov/response-section/burn-restrictions-wildland-fires).
- **Publishing.** The schedule adds 24 polls per day and uses the existing two-minute merge delay. An alert may arrive just after a poll and wait until the next hourly publication. The Worker filters expired CAP messages and legal windows on reads. Weather records bypass sitemap history, status transitions and push/email. The HTTP status and county endpoints and MCP results include optional `alerts`. The new `/api/v1/fire-weather` endpoint lists current signals by county with optional state filtering. [OpenAPI](../public/openapi.json).
- **Verification.** The Bun test exercises the real CAP fixtures, HTTP and MCP output, county-scoped legal timing, the queue, and status history. The local wall-time benchmark measured 0.220 ms p95 for 36 KB of live GeoJSON and 0.812 ms p95 for 135 KB of real archived alerts. Current-source merge and encode measured 7.533 ms p95 versus 7.222 ms on the baseline. The synthetic 51-area projection measured 11.244 ms p95 versus 11.511 ms on the baseline. Cloudflare production CPU requires separate measurement.

## 2026-11-24 Pennsylvania (PA)

- **Restrictions.** Adams is restricted by Carroll Valley Borough's November 20 outdoor open-burning ban, which the borough says lasts until further notice. The other 66 counties are unknown. There are zero countywide bans and zero countywide all-clears. The CivicPlus active category polls four times daily Eastern. The shared `civicPlusNewsFlash` helper reads the category list, checks its article count and parses publication dates. The adapter reads the latest recognized issue or lift with the existing HTTP, HTML and date helpers. It requires renewed confirmation after 14 days for the open-ended notice. [Borough notice](https://www.carrollvalley.gov/CivicAlerts.aspx?AID=16), [active announcements](https://www.carrollvalley.gov/m/newsflash?cat=5).
- **County and statewide orders.** County governing bodies can issue open-fire bans for up to 30 days after the district forester's recommendation and the required fire-chief support. Commissioners can extend a ban for up to 30 more days. Municipalities and land managers have separate authority, and the Governor issues statewide proclamations. PEMA's listing showed no wildfire proclamation. DCNR's AEM page had no state lands order in its guidance block. Its linked PDF's November 1, 06:41 all-clear is stale, and active counties require reading the map color. The registry keeps the map manual. Check its printed date and the county or municipality directly. [DCNR burn bans](https://www.pa.gov/agencies/dcnr/programs-and-services/community-outreach-and-development/wildfire/burn-bans), [Act 52](https://www.palegis.us/statutes/unconsolidated/law-information?ActNum=0052.&SessInd=0&SessYr=1995&plPageNum=0332), [PEMA proclamations](https://www.pa.gov/agencies/pema/resources/emergency-proclamations), [county contacts](https://www.pa.gov/agencies/ready/contact/contact-county).
- **Other candidates and land rules.** Bucks's December 11 and Franklin's November 27 lifts are both from 2024. Lebanon's indexed current-status page says inactive without a date, and both HTTPS hostnames failed certificate-name validation. DEP's standing rules differ inside and outside air basins. The state forest adapter verifies the current code HTML and schedules March 1–May 25 Eastern in `lib/calendar.ts` for state forest lands in 51 counties, with alert suppression. The code also limits fire rings and fireplaces at high, very high or extreme DCNR danger, subject to department permission. The latest observed and forecast maps covered November 22 and 23 and remain human checks. Ask the district forester about current conditions and permission. [Bucks lift](https://www.buckscounty.gov/m/newsflash/home/detail/1180), [Franklin lift](https://www.franklincountypa.gov/franklin-county-commissioners-lift-burn-ban/), [Lebanon status](https://lebcounty.com/ema/burn-ban/), [DEP open burning](https://www.pa.gov/agencies/dep/programs-and-services/air/bureau-of-air-quality/open-burning-information), [DCNR danger maps](https://www.pa.gov/agencies/dcnr/programs-and-services/community-outreach-and-development/wildfire), [captures, PDF hash and county mapping](../src/worker/sources/pa/fixtures/README.md).
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge verified both active sources, the coverage endpoint and all 67 county endpoints. All 189 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Fixture parse p95 was 0.160 ms for Carroll Valley and 0.544 ms for the in-season state forest rule. The existing merge and 51-area benchmarks exceeded the 7 ms review threshold at 8.349 ms and 11.444 ms p95. Every Pennsylvania URL passed both link checks. The first repo-wide run hit an existing Maryland timeout and Tennessee connection reset. The retry had 144 OK, 18 access blocks and one timeout on the existing Sable Altura WordPress feed in Colorado.

## PA county pass (2026-11-24)

The county sweep checked all 67 counties. The publisher captures were taken November 25 Eastern. The fixture-backed map has **59 restricted, 8 unknown, 0 countywide bans and 0 countywide all-clears**. Fifty-eight counties moved from unknown to restricted. Adams already had the Carroll Valley Borough ban. The standing rules apply only to the activities and jurisdictions cited on each result.

- **Carbon County.** The county's ArcGIS app links to a FeatureServer with 23 named municipalities and three explicit statuses. The capture shows Lansford as closed and Nesquehoning, Summit Hill, Lehighton and Palmerton as limited by local ordinance. The county’s FeatureServer item was modified November 16. The service supplies no per-record effective or edit dates. The adapter checks the complete roster, polls three times daily Eastern and publishes positive municipal decisions. [County map](https://carbongis.maps.arcgis.com/apps/webappviewer/index.html?id=b59aeef167da47e8884609b2ee6c926d) · [County layer item](https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=8764723e6b384e1ab248c9ecca7406af)
- **Pike County.** The live WebCAD view reported a ban for Palmyra Township. It was logged November 4 and still listed in the November 24 evening and November 25 afternoon views. The adapter polls hourly and reads the dedicated Burning Bans group with the page refresh time in Eastern. The controlled-burn and road-closure group records dispatch calls. Confirm the written order, exceptions and end date with Palmyra before acting on the report. [Live WebCAD](https://webcad.pikepa.org/pages/public/liveincidents.aspx) · [Pike County jurisdiction](https://court.pikepa.org/about/)
- **Standing rules.** DEP's air-basin definitions cover four entire counties and defined municipalities in another 16. The adapter verifies both the legal rule and its municipality definitions. Philadelphia and Allegheny have local air programs. The 37 local rules use General Code HTML, government and fire-department HTML, or official ordinance PDFs. All rule sources are recurring and positive-only. [DEP operating rule](https://www.pacodeandbulletin.gov/secure/pacode/data/025/chapter129/s129.14.html) · [Publisher captures](../src/worker/sources/pa/fixtures/README.md#county-pass-captures)
- **Other feeds.** The ArcGIS keyword and Pennsylvania-extent searches found Carbon's application and layers. Searches for state or regional KML maps, Nixle and Everbridge listings found no additional publishing feed for current county decisions. The 2024 county notices and rescissions in CivicPlus, WordPress and commission records remain reference material. The DCNR county PDF remains manual under the previous pass.

## 2026-11-24 Idaho (ID)

- **Restrictions.** Kootenai is restricted within the Kootenai County Fire & Rescue district. The DEQ Coeur d'Alene forecast issued November 23 shows Yellow for outdoor burning on November 24. The district prohibits residential yard waste, burn barrels, logging slash, and recreational or cooking fires during Yellow or Red conditions. Its compact ASP.NET MVC forecast uses `lib/http.ts`, `lib/html.ts` and `lib/dates.ts`, including the new weekday/date validator. It polls six times daily Pacific. The other 43 counties are unknown, with zero countywide bans and zero all-clears. Confirm the district boundary and daily hotline color at 800-633-6247 before burning. [DEQ forecast](https://www2.deq.idaho.gov/air/AQIPublic/Forecast?siteId=2) · [DEQ regional ordinance guidance](https://www2.deq.idaho.gov/air/AQIPublic/Ordinance/Index/1) · [District rules](https://kootenaifire.com/burn-permits/)
- **Fire stages and permits.** The current IDL dashboard reads an ArcGIS Enterprise MapServer layer. All 25 zones showed Stage None, with the latest edit on September 11. Both inventoried NIFC FeatureServer layers showed no stages and had April edit dates. BLM posted September lifts for its Coeur d'Alene, Boise, and Twin Falls lands. IDL's interagency plan defines the zone and agency roles. The new `lib/arcgis.ts` consumer reads the current layer four times daily and maps active stages to county intersections as scoped restrictions. Its freshness window uses the zone edit timestamp and any earlier rescission. Idaho's May 10–October 20 closed fire season had ended. The § 38-115 adapter uses `lib/calendar.ts`, local Mountain and Pacific windows, and recurring metadata to suppress alerts. Districts may extend the season or suspend permits. Check the finder and signed order for the location, land manager, and applicable exceptions. [IDL finder](https://www.idl.idaho.gov/fire-management/fire-restrictions-finder/) · [IDL plan](https://www.idl.idaho.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/IDAHO_FIRE_RESTRICTIONS_PLAN_2026.pdf) · [BLM orders](https://www.blm.gov/programs/fire/regional-info/idaho/fire-restrictions) · [Permit portal](https://burnpermits.idaho.gov/) · [Captures and county crosswalk](../src/worker/sources/id/fixtures/README.md)
- **Other candidates.** All 12 inventory URLs responded with HTTP 200. The manual 9.5 MB DEQ GeoJSON showed 34 explicit Green messages, five Green entries without messages, and five voluntary Yellow weather advisories in Benewah, Bonner, Boundary, Kootenai, and Shoshone. It carries no decision dates, and the HTML map's update heading changed with the request time. DEQ can issue binding air-quality restrictions under IDAPA 58.01.01.557, and local or tribal restrictions also apply. The open-burning and prescribed-fire pages explain the rules. The crop-residue map displayed a November 23 preliminary update and lists individual requests and approvals. Check those maps or contact the local authority for activities and places outside the automated scopes. [DEQ map](https://www2.deq.idaho.gov/air/AQIPublic/Map/OutdoorBurn) · [Can I Burn?](https://www.deq.idaho.gov/air-quality/smoke-and-burning/can-i-burn/) · [Prescribed fire](https://www.deq.idaho.gov/air-quality/smoke-and-burning/prescribed-fire/) · [Crop-residue decisions](https://www2.deq.idaho.gov/air/CRB/BurnDecisionMap/Index)
- **Verification.** The local hourly queue checked all three feeds and published the district restriction. Coverage and all 44 county endpoints agreed. All 199 Bun tests passed, including the Idaho p95 CPU tests below 5 ms, and TypeScript passed. Fixture wall-time p95 was 0.113 ms for IDL, 0.262 ms for DEQ and 0.328 ms for the permit season. The current-source merge and synthetic 51-area run were 7.822 and 11.129 ms p95, above the existing 7 ms review threshold. All Idaho links passed. The sitewide first run had a Lane County wood-heating timeout and Tennessee connection reset. The retry had 152 OK, 17 access blocks (including Tennessee), and zero failures.

## 2026-11-24 Alaska (AK)

- **Restrictions.** Fairbanks North Star Borough and Juneau are restricted within the Fairbanks and North Pole PM2.5 nonattainment area and the Mendenhall Valley wood smoke control area, respectively. The other 28 county equivalents are unknown. There are zero countywide bans and zero all-clears. DEC prohibits open burning in those designated areas from November 1 through March 31 and can issue additional air quality restrictions. Its indexed North Pole Stage 2 notice was scheduled through 2 p.m. Alaska time on November 24. The alert table, notice and DEC guidance returned DataDome 403s over direct GET. The advisory URL passed one link-check retry, then three isolated fetches returned 403. Keep the alert feed manual. Check the DEC maps and watch the proposed Fairbanks–North Pole area split before updating the Fairbanks mapping. [Winter rule and maps](https://dec.alaska.gov/air/burnwise/open-burn-prohibition), [DEC advisories](https://dec.alaska.gov/Applications/Air/airtoolsweb/advisories/), [proposed split](https://dec.alaska.gov/air/anpms/communities/fbks-pm2-5-nonattainment-area-split-faq/).
- **Forestry.** The commissioner can extend the April 1–August 31 fire season, suspend permits in a zone or order emergency closures. The regular 2026 fire season had ended, and no extension was confirmed. The ArcGIS FeatureServer returned 22 zones, with 15 no-permit, six open-with-permit and one blank code. Its last edit was September 15 and there is no per-zone decision timestamp. The fetcher polls four times daily and requires an edit within 48 hours to publish a closure or suspension. The geometry intersects 29 county equivalents. The June and August WordPress notices are historical. For current local conditions, use the DNR area page or permit hotline. [Permit map](https://dnr.alaska.gov/burn/fireareas), [DNR FAQ](https://dnr.alaska.gov/burn/faq), [source captures and geography](../src/worker/sources/ak/fixtures/README.md).
- **Calendar and local rules.** The two Alaska BASIS sources verify the current published text and use the shared calendar helper for the winter air-quality ban and the state permit season. Both are recurring and exclude scheduled changes from alerts. The shared HTTP helper now spaces a publisher's sequential requests and recognizes DataDome responses. The Anchorage Fire Department displayed Low Fire Danger with no decision timestamp. Its hotline is 907-267-5020. BLM's listed Gilmore and Gold Run order expired October 31. Check municipal rules, tribal authorities and land managers for their own orders. [Anchorage recreational fire status](https://www.muni.org/departments/fire/wildfire_home/is_today_a_burn_day.php), [Juneau permits](https://juneau.org/fire/burn-permit), [BLM orders](https://www.blm.gov/programs/fire-and-aviation/regional-info/alaska-fire-service/fire-restrictions).
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge published the two winter restrictions. The coverage, active bans and all 30 county endpoints agreed. Bun passed all 222 tests and TypeScript passed. The three Alaska parser CPU tests stayed below 5 ms p95. Fixture wall time p95 was 0.075 ms for ArcGIS, 0.405 ms for the permit calendar and 0.742 ms for the winter rule. The existing 51-area projection remained above the review threshold at 10.347 ms p95. The link checker found no Alaska failures apart from access blocks. On retry it reported 159 OK, 18 blocked, a Washington Northwest Clean Air Agency timeout and a Tennessee burn-ban connection reset. The two earlier Lane County timeouts cleared.
