Many are advance-call or approval requirements. McKenzie has a signed open-burning ban for the area covered by its order. Cass requires High fire danger or a Red Flag Warning to activate its April order. There was no active Red Flag Warning in North Dakota. [ND Response map](https://burnrestrictions.nd.gov) · [ND Response map guide](https://www.ndresponse.gov/burn-restrictions-fire-danger-maps) · [Signed orders and captures](../src/worker/sources/nd/fixtures/README.md)
- **Authority.** County and city executives declare local emergencies under NDCC 37-17.1-10, with governing-board consent beyond seven days. The governor can issue statewide orders. Tribes and land managers issue restrictions for their jurisdictions. Executive Order 2026-05 activates the statewide emergency response through December 1. It establishes no statewide burn prohibition. DEQ regulates open burning and variances under NDAC 33.1-15-04, including a prohibition on covered open burning at Extreme fire danger. Check the current map and the county, city, tribe or land manager for the location and activity. [NDCC 37-17.1](https://ndlegis.gov/cencode/t37c17-1.pdf) · [Governor's order](https://www.governor.nd.gov/sites/default/files/documents/Executive%20Order%202026-05%20statewide%20fire%20emergency.pdf) · [DEQ open-burning rules](https://deq.nd.gov/aq/permitting/OpenBurning.aspx) · [Emergency managers](https://www.des.nd.gov/countytribal-contacts)
- **ArcGIS FeatureServer and Dashboard WebMaps.** The hourly positive-only adapter joins the public declarations to the separate issued fire-danger report and checks activity thresholds, dates, rescissions and dated warnings with shared ArcGIS, date and paced HTTP helpers. The declarations had refreshed on November 24. The separate fire-danger feed was still dated November 23 at capture, so the parser used requirements that apply at every rating. The shared ArcGIS helper now parses offset-bearing dates. The existing NWS supplement shares the declaration validity check for Cass, McLean and Mercer. The map omits some local orders and has some activity and boundary entries that differ from signed orders. Check the signed order for boundaries, exemptions and the local rating in Divide. [Map implementation and exact source requests](../src/worker/sources/nd/fixtures/README.md)
- **Access.** The Cass County news and PDF inventory URLs returned Akamai 403 on direct GET. A copy of the signed April 6 declaration was available on the original state WebEOC FeatureServer. The other inventory URLs returned HTTP 200. DES's HTML explains the map and its January 2026 guidance explains local emergency declarations, activity exceptions and reporting to the state. [Cass signed declaration on DES](https://nddesgis.nd.gov/server/rest/services/Hosted/WebEOC_Burn_Restriciions_Publish/FeatureServer/0/31/attachments/82) · [DES guidance](https://www.des.nd.gov/sites/default/files/documents/docs/2026-0120_BurnRestrictionsGuidance.pdf)
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge published the 22 scoped results. Coverage, active restrictions, all 53 county API responses and the McKenzie County page agreed. All 262 Bun tests passed, and TypeScript and the site build passed. The ND fixture parsed in 1.736 ms p95, below the 5 ms fixture test limit. The current-source merge and synthetic 51-area projection were 7.692 and 10.535 ms p95 against the existing 7 ms review threshold. The first link check found a Douglas County, Colorado timeout and a Tennessee connection reset. The retry passed with 186 OK, 21 access blocks and zero failures. Douglas passed, Tennessee returned HTTP 403 and Cass remained blocked.

## 2026-11-24 Nevada (NV)

- **Restrictions.** Two scoped county results, Clark and Washoe. The Truckee Meadows district reports pile burning is open with an approved permit and suitable weather. The Forest Service's current table lists the Spring Mountains campfire order for mapped National Forest lands near communities in Clark County. The other 15 county equivalents are unknown. There are zero countywide bans and zero all-clears. [Daily district notice](https://tmfpd.us/outdoor-burning/?burn-status-check=1) · [Forest order](https://www.fs.usda.gov/r04/humboldt-toiyabe/alerts/year-round-fire-restrictions-spring-mountains-national-recreation-area)
- **Authority.** Nevada's State Forester Firewarden posts and lifts fire orders in its jurisdiction. The state permit statute applies outside incorporated cities and towns, with exceptions. Federal and tribal managers issue separate orders for their lands. Local fire authorities administer permits and closures, and Nevada DEP and local air-quality agencies regulate smoke and eligible types of burning. The state's prescribed-burn map tracks permits and operations. Check the local permit issuer and the manager of the parcel before burning. [Nevada fire statutes](https://www.leg.state.nv.us/NRS/NRS-472.html#NRS472Sec510) · [State lands orders](https://forestry.nv.gov/wildland-fire-management/fire-incidents-restrictions) · [Smoke and permitting rules](https://ndep.nv.gov/air/air-pollutants/smoke-management)
- **WordPress/Oxygen and Drupal.** Both adapters use shared HTTP, HTML and Pacific date helpers in positive-only mode. The district polls six times daily and expires the banner at local midnight. Its bare URL served an old cache, and REST returned empty content. The Forest Service polls daily, validates the current table and exact order and map links, and stops at the signed cutoff or after 120 days without a publisher page update. The shared HTML table reader now preserves separation between paragraphs in a cell. [Exact requests, scope and captures](../src/worker/sources/nv/fixtures/README.md)
- **Manual checks.** Nevada Fire Info's ArcGIS layer was last edited October 5 and still showed Stage 1 for Winnemucca and Battle Mountain after their signed October 31 end dates. The BLM Sierra Front notice describes August 31 Stage 2. BLM lifted its Carson City District order September 21, and Truckee Meadows lifted its temporary fire restrictions November 1. The signed Western Region state-land order is still listed and says it runs until rescinded. Ask the state forester to confirm its current boundaries, especially the Washoe Lake State Park reference. The Forest Service lists other scoped Carson Ranger District occupancy orders. Confirm Mount Stirling's Nye County boundary with the Forest Service before mapping it. Great Basin National Park's November 18 conditions page lists year-round rules while the map still shows the summer Stage 2 order. Check with the park for the current order. [Interagency order index](https://www.nevadafireinfo.org/restrictions) · [BLM Sierra Front notice](https://www.blm.gov/announcement/stage-2-fire-restrictions-take-effect-sierra-front) · [Western Region order](https://www.nevadafireinfo.org/s/Western-Region-Restrictions-2026-lzj5.pdf) · [Forest Service alerts](https://www.fs.usda.gov/r04/humboldt-toiyabe/alerts) · [Great Basin conditions](https://www.nps.gov/grba/planyourvisit/conditions.htm)
- **Air-quality access.** Both inventoried Northern Nevada Public Health URLs failed normal TLS validation because the server omitted an intermediate certificate. The AQMD page displayed Green without a dated decision. Washoe Life's November 2 seasonal announcement and its WordPress feed give the program's rules and hotline. The burn code covers designated ZIP codes and wood-burning devices. Call 775-785-4110 for the daily decision. [Washoe announcement and hotline](https://washoelife.washoecounty.gov/nnph/daily-burn-code-for-wood-burning-devices-is-underway/) · [AQMD status page](https://www.nnph.org/programs-and-services/air-quality/index.php)
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge verified both fetchers, coverage, the active-restrictions feed, all 17 county API responses and the Clark and Washoe pages. All 274 Bun tests, TypeScript and the site build passed. Fixture parse p95 was 0.105 ms for Truckee Meadows and 0.391 ms for the Forest Service. The captured full responses took 1.169 ms and 2.795 ms p95. The current-source merge and synthetic 51-area projection reached 8.929 ms and 10.460 ms p95 against the guide's 7 ms review threshold. The final link check reported 194 OK, 20 access blocks outside Nevada and zero failures. Earlier runs timed out on two Nevada agency links that passed on retry and hit a connection reset on the existing Tennessee page, which returned HTTP 403 on the final run.

## 2026-11-24 Arkansas (AR)

- **Restrictions.** Faulkner County is restricted for an outdoor-burning ban in unincorporated areas. Order 26-204 took effect November 18 at 9 a.m. Central and runs until lifted, with written permits available in special circumstances. Order 26-202 lifted only the earlier 26-170. The other 74 counties are unknown in the automated feed. County judges issue and lift county orders under local emergency authority. Cities and public-land managers can have separate rules. Arkansas DEQ’s year-round open-burning rules cover waste and exemptions. Check the signed order and the local fire authority for the location and activity. [Faulkner order](https://www.faulknercountyar.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/Faulkner-County-Court-Order-26-204.pdf) · [Local emergency authority](https://www.arkleg.state.ar.us/Home/FTPDocument?path=%2FACTS%2F2007%2FPublic%2FACT465.pdf) · [DEQ rules](https://www.adeq.state.ar.us/regs/files/car/8CARpt.40.pdf)
- **WordPress / Elementor and PDF.** The adapter uses `lib/http.ts`, `lib/dates.ts`, scoped HTML extraction and `lib/pdf.ts`. The shared HTML grid helper now preserves the href of a selected link. The county homepage links the active scan. WordPress REST search shows older news, and the home page REST payload omits the header. Older January, August and 2023 scans confirm historical orders only. The adapter polls four times daily Central. Review new scans and rescissions for scope and the order number before accepting them. [County homepage](https://www.faulknercountyar.gov/) · [Exact requests and captures](../src/worker/sources/ar/fixtures/README.md)
- **Manual sources.** The Forestry Division’s AgTools report listed 34 county orders as of November 23 at 4:14 p.m. Central. Curl retrieved it after supplying the missing Sectigo intermediate locally with certificate and hostname validation. Ordinary curl and the local Worker rejected the incomplete chain. The legacy ArkFireInfo link redirects to the same host. The state’s ArcGIS BurnBanCounties service requires a token. Benton County’s Fire Marshal page returned a Cloudflare 403. Pulaski’s March 2025 notice is historical, and Sebastian’s October 5 lift URL returned a DNN archive without the notice body. Ask the county judge for an active signed order and its area. Recheck the state report after the publisher fixes TLS. [Forestry Division](https://agriculture.arkansas.gov/forests/) · [Benton Fire Marshal](https://bentoncountyar.gov/fire-marshal/burn-ban-information/) · [Pulaski notice](https://www.pulaskicounty.net/news/posts/burn-ban-in-effect-for-unincorporated-pulaski-county-until-further-notice)
- **Verification.** The local cron and delayed merge published the scoped restriction. The coverage and active-restrictions feeds, all 75 county API responses, and the Faulkner County page agreed. All 279 Bun tests, TypeScript and the site build passed. Faulkner fixture parsing and PDF hashing took 1.91 ms p95. The full homepage and PDF took 2.75 ms p95. The existing merge and 51-area projection took 8.21 ms and 10.55 ms p95, above the guide’s 7 ms review threshold. The final link check found 195 OK, 21 access blocks and two failures. Both runs hit the Arkansas Forestry TLS error and a connection reset on the existing Tennessee burn-ban page.

## AR county pass (2026-11-24)

The local Worker map showed coverage for 18 of 75 counties. Seventeen moved from unknown, with **5 bans** (Little River, Lonoke, Miller, Union and Van Buren), **11 scoped restrictions** (Benton, Boone, Crawford, Garland, Greene, Howard, Jefferson, Pope, Pulaski, Saline and Washington) and **1 explicit no active county order** (Baxter). Faulkner already had a scoped restriction. The remaining 57 county statuses are unknown.

- Standard TLS validation still fails at Forestry's AgTools county list. A curl call using the system trust store plus the missing [Sectigo OV R36 intermediate](http://crt.sectigo.com/SectigoPublicServerAuthenticationCAOVR36.crt) verified the hostname and certificate and read 34 reported county orders stamped November 24 at 7:31 a.m. Central. The page lists positives and has no order-level scope or lift dates. The old ArkFireInfo route and plain HTTP lead to the same host. The consolidated page embeds a generated PNG. The public ArcGIS `BurnBanYesNo/29` returned 75 county features with every `activeburnban` field null and a March 3 edit timestamp. The separate `BurnBanCounties` service returned ArcGIS 499 Token Required. The Worker keeps Forestry manual. [AgTools list](https://mip.agri.arkansas.gov/agtools/Forestry/Fire_Info/Burn_Bans) · [Public ArcGIS layer](https://adaesri.aad.arkansas.gov/server/rest/services/Hosted/BurnBanYesNo/FeatureServer/29) · [Token-gated layer](https://adaesri.aad.arkansas.gov/server/rest/services/Hosted/BurnBanCounties/FeatureServer)
- Twelve status fetchers poll county and city authorities across HTML banners and tables, CivicPlus alerts, WordPress REST or signed PDF, public JSON, Apptegy, Google Sites and PermitWeb. Baxter's county flag supplies the only full source in this pass. All other live sources use positive-only mode, including Lonoke's timestamped copy of the Forestry list. The seven standing city rules use the reviewed calendar helper and are marked recurring. Each positive city or district report keeps its jurisdiction in the county result. [Request and fixture details](../src/worker/sources/ar/fixtures/README.md)
- Benton CENCOM's `getStatus.php` returned JSON `false` through curl, meaning the portal was open for its participating dispatch districts. Both a scheduled local Worker fetch and a direct retry failed with a connection reset during TLS. The portal is manual and the Rogers permit rule covers the city. The county Fire Marshal page returned a Cloudflare 403. Several county registration and social pages direct residents to broadcast notices. Facebook, X and Nextdoor were treated as human-only links. [CENCOM portal](https://gis.bentoncountyar.gov/controlledburn/) · [Fire Marshal](https://bentoncountyar.gov/fire-marshal/burn-ban-information/)
- All 75 counties were checked through the Association directory and local searches for judge orders, emergency management, fire services, permit pages and alerts. The results below record the strongest local lead. Historical orders and contact-only sources provide no current decision. [Association directory](https://arcounties.org/counties/)
- The delayed local cron published the map at 11:43 p.m. Central. Coverage and representative county status API responses agreed with the map. The final run passed 640 Bun tests and TypeScript. The new fixtures passed the 4 ms p95 thread CPU test, with wall-time p95 between 0.020 ms and 1.873 ms. All new automated links passed. The sitewide link check reported 694 OK, 41 access blocks and one failure on the existing Forestry TLS chain. The repository-wide merge and 51-area projection still flagged their 7 ms review thresholds at 32.416 ms and 10.955 ms p95.

## 2026-11-24 South Carolina (SC)

- **Restrictions.** At the pre-9 a.m. Eastern check, state air-quality rules produced an activity-specific restriction in each of the 46 counties. No countywide bans or all-clears were verified. Regulation 61-62.2 limits when covered land-clearing and right-of-way burns can start and take more fuel. It also closes the residential construction waste exception April 1–October 30 and limits burning to 9 a.m.–3 p.m. Eastern outside that season. In November, these two scheduled restrictions are inactive from 9 a.m. to 3 p.m. Eastern, and countywide status is unknown without another verified report. [SC DES open burning](https://des.sc.gov/programs/bureau-air-quality/air-pollutants/open-burning) · [Regulation PDF](https://des.sc.gov/sites/des/files/Library/Regulations/R.61-62.2.pdf)
- **Authority and notices.** The State Forester and Governor can issue temporary bans covering specified unincorporated areas. Cities and counties can issue local orders. State Red Flag Fire Alerts ask people to postpone burning voluntarily and may trigger specific local ordinances. State law also requires advance notification for covered outdoor burning outside city limits. SCFC's March 27 ban lifted March 30. The April 17 ban lifted in stages on April 29 and 30, with the remaining 27 counties lifted May 1 at 7 a.m. Eastern. The older 2025 PDF describes a partial lift only. Horry's two inventory notices lifted local orders January 13 and May 4. Its archive showed no later burn-ban entry, and its Fire Rescue guide gives standing rules. Ask the local fire authority about the address and activity. [State statute](https://www.scstatehouse.gov/code/t48c035.php) · [Governor authority](https://www.scstatehouse.gov/code/t48c031.php) · [SCFC definitions](https://www.scfc.gov/protection/fire-burning/fire-resources/bans-alerts/) · [SCFC releases](https://www.scfc.gov/news-releases/) · [Horry guidance](https://www.horrycountysc.gov/departments/fire-rescue/open-burning/)
- **WordPress custom REST and regulation PDF.** The read-only form lookup returned 46 `--no` flags dated November 16–23. The form inserts the visitor's current date in its display. Automation polls three administrative regions at 10 a.m., 2 p.m. and 6 p.m. Eastern, spaces lookups by one second and only publishes same-day affirmative flags for residential yard debris outside cities and towns. The reusable HTTP reader now accepts per-request bodies, and the shared WordPress URL helper accepts plugin namespaces. The DES source uses the shared PDF hash and Eastern calendar helpers and is marked recurring. [SCFC county selector](https://www.scfc.gov/notify/) · [Exact requests and captures](../src/worker/sources/sc/fixtures/README.md)
- **Manual and access.** All eight inventory URLs returned HTTP 200. The custom bulk county route returned HTTP 500. The generic WordPress status-message list contains templates from 2023, and the release page links PDF event notices. The maps show burn notifications and fire incidents. The fiResponse restriction endpoint returned an empty list without a status date. The Horry Municode link returned Cloudflare 403. Use the SCFC release PDFs and Horry Fire Rescue to confirm current orders, lifts, boundaries and exemptions. Ask SCFC whether a supported aggregate current-status feed is available. [SCFC bulk route](https://www.scfc.gov/wp-json/scfcapi/v2/counties) · [SCFC app](https://www.scfc.gov/protection/fire-burning/mobile-wildfire-app/) · [Horry public safety archive](https://www.horrycountysc.gov/news/public-safety)
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron, exercised at the 10:07 a.m. Eastern schedule slot, completed all four SC polls and published the snapshot at 5:56 a.m. Eastern real time. Coverage, the active-restrictions API, all 46 county API responses and the Horry County page agreed. All 287 Bun tests, TypeScript and the site build passed. SC fixture p95 was 0.181 ms for the 46 WordPress responses and 0.478 ms for the regulation PDF and calendar. The current-source merge and synthetic 51-area run took 9.120 ms and 10.630 ms p95 against the guide’s 7 ms review threshold. The local published snapshot was 1,513,014 bytes. All SC links in the rebuilt site and registry passed. The final link check reported 204 OK, 19 access blocks and two failures. Arkansas Forestry had a TLS certificate-chain error and the Tennessee page reset the connection. The initial Oregon LRAPA timeout cleared on retry.

## 2026-11-24 North Carolina (NC)

- **Restrictions.** The morning check found activity-specific restrictions in all 100 counties, with zero countywide bans, zero all-clears, and zero unknown summaries at that hour. Woodland permits apply all day in 21 high-hazard counties and from midnight to 4 p.m. Eastern in the other 79 for covered fires in or within 500 feet of protected woods. The state air-quality rule adds limits on starting or fueling certain burns outside 8 a.m.–6 p.m. Eastern. From 4 to 6 p.m. Eastern, the recurring feeds alone report 21 restricted and 79 unknown. Both rule sources use the shared calendar helper and suppress scheduled alerts. [N.C. General Statutes, Article 78](https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/ByArticle/Chapter_106/Article_78.html) · [NC DEQ open burning](https://www.deq.nc.gov/about/divisions/air-quality/air-quality-compliance/open-burning)
- **Orders.** The Agriculture Commissioner issues and lifts state orders through the N.C. Forest Service. Local fire marshals and towns can issue their own orders, including near occupied dwellings. The March 28 state order lifted for 81 counties May 3 and for the remaining 19 May 8. The September 23 caution was voluntary, and no newer state order appeared in the newsroom. The Drupal adapter polls six times daily Eastern and uses shared HTTP pacing, HTML extraction and date validation. It publishes explicit orders beyond 100 feet of occupied dwellings, pairs lifts by county and time, and requires a current order within its 60-day archive window. Check the newsroom directly for older unresolved orders or unfamiliar formats. A lift leaves countywide status unconfirmed. [NCFS newsroom](https://www.ncagr.gov/divisions/nc-forest-service/newsroom) · [May 3 lift](https://www.ncagr.gov/news/press-releases/2026/05/02/states-ban-open-burning-lifted-81-north-carolina-counties-conditions-improve-recent-rain) · [May 8 lift](https://www.ncagr.gov/news/press-releases/2026/05/07/state-issued-burn-ban-lifted-all-north-carolina-counties-fire-danger-moderates-following-recent)
- **Manual checks.** The permit portal offers applications, and the ArcGIS burn-ban layer still has 2019 flags. The November 24 air-quality map had 100 county `No` flags for its own rule. Its flags omit hours and historical maps missed action days described in the discussion, so the forecast remains manual. Chatham and Greensboro returned Akamai 403s, and Buncombe returned a Cloudflare 403. For a specific address and activity, check the forecast discussion and ask the county fire marshal, county ranger, or land manager. [Forecast discussion](https://airquality.climate.ncsu.edu/discussion/) · [County rangers](https://www.ncagr.gov/divisions/nc-forest-service/contacts) · [Fire marshal directory](https://www.ncosfm.gov/nc-fire-department-rescue-ems-directory-including-fdid) · [Source inventory results and dated captures](../src/worker/sources/nc/fixtures/README.md)
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge completed all three NC polls. At 7:08 a.m. Eastern the coverage API, active-restrictions feed, all 100 county API responses, and the Wake County page agreed. Bun passed 301 tests, TypeScript passed, and the site built. Fixture p95 was 2.03 ms for the newsroom and orders, 0.63 ms for the statute, and 0.30 ms for the air-quality hours. The repository-wide merge and 51-area projection reached 12.26 and 9.95 ms p95 against the 7 ms review threshold. All shipped NC links passed. The final sitewide check found 210 OK, 20 access blocks, and two failures. The Arkansas Forestry certificate chain failed validation and the Tennessee burn-ban page reset the connection. The first run's Oregon LRAPA timeout cleared on retry.

## 2026-11-24 Missouri (MO)

- **Restrictions.** Show Me Fire reports one active county ban in Dallas, effective October 11 at 12:41 p.m. Central until further notice. Its review form asks for official proof, and Dallas's public record has no signed-order link. The federal yard-waste rule adds scoped restrictions in Franklin, Jefferson, St. Charles and St. Louis counties and the independent City of St. Louis. Missouri's 115 county equivalents yield one reported ban, five restricted, 109 unknown and zero all-clears. Confirm the Dallas order and any lift with the county commission or emergency management. [Show Me Fire](https://showmefire.org/burn-bans) · [Dallas County contacts](https://www.dallascountymo-oem.gov/community-resources-contact-info)
- **Authority and state reports.** Under RSMo §49.266, the county commission may issue an order after a State Fire Marshal determination based on a major disaster and severe or worse drought. Agricultural burning using best management practices and state fire-management activities have exemptions under that section. Municipalities and tax-supported fire districts can issue their own local orders. The November 23 SEMA PDF reproduced the Show Me Fire map with one active county. The September 16 DFS workbook listed open-ended Lawrence, Ozark and Newton bans, a McDonald order with a September 26 end date, and unresolved inquiries or older notes. The brief and workbook remain manual references. Check the local issuer for current boundaries, exceptions and lifts, and DNR for statewide air-quality and material rules. [RSMo §49.266](https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=49.266) · [SEMA brief](https://sema.dps.mo.gov/newspubs/docs/dailybriefs.pdf) · [DFS workbook and authority](https://dfs.dps.mo.gov/programs/resources/county-burn-bans.php) · [DNR open-burning rules](https://dnr.mo.gov/document-search/facts-open-burning-under-missouri-regulations-pub2047/pub2047)
- **Fetchers and access.** The hourly Nuxt JSON source uses shared HTTP and date helpers and expires reports by the map timestamp and each order's stated end. The ArcGIS overlay returned the same active record and 114 inactive placeholders. The Drupal EPA index and PDFs verify the approved rule and metro boundary before the daily Central calendar applies the April 15–September 15 closure or the in-season limits. Its recurring metadata suppresses alerts. St. Louis County's PDF describes separate limits on recreational fires and lot-specific yard-waste rules. Wentzville stopped requiring its own open-burn permits in September 2025 and lists standing district limits. All five inventory URLs returned HTTP 200. The county's Municode page returned Cloudflare 403, and direct eCFR requests reached a CAPTCHA. EPA's approval table and PDFs were accessible. [Exact captures and stack details](../src/worker/sources/mo/fixtures/README.md) · [St. Louis County fact sheet](https://my.stlouiscountymo.gov/st-louis-county-departments/public-health/environmental-services/air-pollution-control/open-burning-information1/) · [Wentzville district guidance](https://www.wentzvillefire.org/residential-burn-permit)
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge published both sources at 6:24 a.m. Central. Coverage, the active list, all 115 county API responses and the Dallas and St. Louis county pages agreed. All 309 Bun tests passed, TypeScript passed, and the site built. Fixture p95 was 0.039 ms for the JSON feed and 2.832 ms for the EPA index and PDFs. The combined CPU test stayed below 4 ms p95. The sitewide merge and 51-area projection took 11.319 and 10.333 ms p95 against the 7 ms review threshold. Every shipped Missouri link passed. The sitewide check had 219 OK, 20 access blocks and one failure from Arkansas Forestry's existing TLS certificate chain.

The November 24 fixture merge moved **56 of 109** unknown county equivalents to **restricted**. Missouri totals were **1 ban, 61 restricted, 53 unknown and 0 all-clears**. The additions are city or fire-district reports and restrictions. The last verified statewide pass supplies the existing Dallas report and five St. Louis metro restrictions.

- **Daily decisions.** Cape Girardeau uses an Elementor notice with the decision date and posted a no-burn day on November 24. The city limits cross Cape Girardeau and Scott counties. Lake Ozark Fire Protection District uses a Wix banner and posted a Burn Day for its district in Camden and Miller counties. Both adapters require the current Central date and expire any closure at local midnight. A permissive daily banner yields no countywide all-clear. The Lake Ozark rules still apply on burn days. ## 2026-11-24 Tennessee (TN)

- **Restrictions.** The November 24 morning check found scoped restrictions in 92 of 95 counties. Davidson, Knox and Shelby remain unknown in the automated summaries, and the Division of Forestry directs residents to local authorities there. Zero countywide bans or all-clears were verified. The state permit system showed temporary hazardous-fire-weather holds in Claiborne, Grainger, Hancock, Jackson and Putnam. Sevier's newly posted hold starts November 25. The recurring October 15–May 15 permit requirement covers debris and prescribed fires near vegetation where the state permit program applies. [Permit map](https://tn.firesponse.com/burn-permit/public-restrictions/) · [Permit season](https://www.tn.gov/tnwildlandfire/prevention/safe-debris-burning/permits.html) · [Local authority directory](https://www.tn.gov/tnwildlandfire/prevention/safe-debris-burning/permits/county-municipality-list.html)
- **Authority and sources.** The Agriculture Commissioner can issue burn bans in extreme fire conditions in consultation with the State Forester and county mayors. The Governor can issue regional or statewide bans. Municipal ordinances, local orders, air-quality rules and land-manager restrictions also affect whether and where a fire is allowed. The hourly fiResponse Nuxt JSON adapter follows the public map's dated jurisdiction query with the county details query. The unfiltered response gave 160 jurisdictions without decision dates or reasons. The dated details distinguish temporary holds from standing referrals and carry start and end times. The calendar uses a daily Adobe AEM guidance check, both Tennessee time zones and `recurring: true`. The shared date helper parses offset-free timestamps, and the calendar helper supports opening-day hours and carries continuous windows across midnight. [Exact requests and fixtures](../src/worker/sources/tn/fixtures/README.md) · [State wildfire laws](https://www.tn.gov/tnwildlandfire/prevention/safe-debris-burning/tn-wildfire-fire-laws.html) · [TDEC open burning](https://www.tn.gov/environment/air/open-burning.html)
- **Manual checks and access.** The ArcGIS item and web map resolve to the county FeatureServer with 95 `None` flags, a March 27, 2024 data edit and an April 2026 schema edit. The state's burn-ban guidance showed a no-active-bans statement in the search index without a decision date. Direct GETs returned 403 or a connection reset. Rutherford's three GovOffice pages were reachable and describe October 2023 and August 2024 bans and the November 2023 lift. A separate September 2024 notice lifts the August order. The archive has no current decision. Shelby's inventory PDF returned Cloudflare 403. The CivicPlus health page and code index were reachable and describe standing rules and exceptions. Ask Forestry about current Commissioner orders and the local fire or air-quality authority about the address and activity. [ArcGIS dashboard](https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=1e74706ced2c4062b5fc557ddd24617a) · [Rutherford Fire Marshal](https://rutherfordcountytn.gov/fire-marshal) · [September 2024 lift](https://rutherfordcountytn.gov/index.asp?DE=BFE5AF5C-E373-4592-B7F2-041289413253&SEC=763D13EF-A556-49BB-B17F-430551ED58A0) · [Shelby air code](https://www.shelbytnhealth.com/169/Air-Pollution-Control-Code)
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge published both feeds at 6:43 a.m. Central. Coverage, the active list, all 95 county API responses and the Claiborne County page agreed. All 317 Bun tests passed, TypeScript passed and the site built. The two fixtures took 0.459 and 0.248 ms p95 wall time, with combined thread CPU of 1.992 ms p95. The repository-wide merge and synthetic projection still exceed the 7 ms review threshold at 12.098 and 10.206 ms p95. The final sitewide link check had 221 OK, 27 access blocks and two failures on existing Arkansas URLs. The portal timeout cleared on a direct retry and the Forestry TLS chain still failed. Tennessee's official pages intermittently reset or returned 403, including the permit guidance that did succeed in the local Worker. Check the guidance from the production Worker before rollout.

## TN county pass (2026-11-24)

- **Map coverage.** Replaying the captured official pages produced local restrictions in 34 counties. Combined with the existing seasonal permit rule on a simulated Tennessee HTTP 403, all 95 summaries were restricted on November 24. That moves 88 of 95 from the supplied live unknown baseline to restricted in the replay. Countywide bans and all-clears both totaled zero. The seasonal fallback covers 92 counties and identifies its last manual verification in the county note. Davidson, Knox and Shelby have their own local rules. [Capture and replay evidence](../src/worker/sources/tn/fixtures/README.md)
- **Local status sources.** Hamilton’s Elementor widget gave permitted hours that day. Tullahoma’s Revize page still displayed a Google Forms embed, and the form said weather had suspended applications in the city. The status applies to the city in both Coffee and Franklin counties. Gatlinburg’s Apptegy notice verified its permit pause through November 22. The Memphis and Knoxville AirNow forecasts gave no permit holds in the November 24 capture. The September Memphis captures verified a Code Orange branch. [Hamilton bulletin](https://apcb.org/) · [Tullahoma permits](https://www.tullahomatn.gov/departments/fire_department/burn_permit.php) · [Gatlinburg notice](https://www.gatlinburgtn.gov/article/3354138) · [AirNow](https://www.airnow.gov/)
- **Standing rules.** Thirty-three reviewed county, municipal and fire-department sources add local permit, activity or hours limits. The main groups are CivicPlus, WordPress, GovOffice, Revize, Wix, MTAS municipal codes and official PDFs. Each uses positive-only mode and a recurring rule. The PDF, HTML and transport helpers distinguish a changed document from an access outage. Alamo and Pulaski have larger municipal code PDFs than the current source size limit. Their relevant sections are listed for manual review.
- **Sweep.** The CTAS roster supplied all 95 county starting points. The pass checked 339 linked department, alert, newsroom and commission pages, plus direct permit, fire district and ordinance leads. “Nothing found” means no separate local burn status or standing rule was found in the sources checked. Enrollments and unconfirmed history are listed as human links. Facebook links point to the agencies’ own public pages. [CTAS county directory](https://www.ctas.tennessee.edu/county-directory)
- **Access.** The four existing `tn.gov` links and the legacy Shelby unincorporated-area PDF returned HTTP 403 in the link checker. The Dyersburg Fire Codes and Alert Center also returned HTTP 403 when checked directly. The old Rhea and White county directory destinations blocked access, and the Henry directory domain returned HTTP 511. Alternate fire or city pages are listed where reachable. Sumner’s Everbridge and Weakley’s Nixle links lead to login pages. The statewide ArcGIS layer remains stale as recorded in the earlier pass. [Legacy Shelby PDF](https://www.shelbycountytn.gov/DocumentCenter/View/883/open_burn_rules) · [Dyersburg Alert Center](https://www.dyersburgtn.gov/AlertCenter.aspx)
- **Verification.** All 551 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Dedicated fixture tests passed their 4 ms p95 thread CPU limits. Fixture wall p95 was 0.19 ms for Hamilton, 0.21 ms for Tullahoma, 0.55 ms for Gatlinburg and 0.12 ms for AirNow. Reviewed rules ranged from 0.16 to 5.97 ms wall p95, with the Tiptonville code PDF at the high end. The existing aggregate benchmarks reported budget warnings at 20.26 and 10.04 ms p95. The link checker reported 607 OK, 35 blocked and one existing Arkansas Forestry TLS-chain failure across 643 URLs. Every new Tennessee registry link passed. [Arkansas Forestry error target](https://mip.agri.arkansas.gov/agtools/Forestry/Fire_Info/Burn_Bans)

## 2026-11-24 New York (NY)

- **Restrictions.** On November 24, 21 of 62 counties had scoped restrictions, 41 were unknown, and zero had a verified countywide ban or all-clear. DEC's Fire Town permits cover designated towns in Clinton, Delaware, Essex, Franklin, Fulton, Greene, Hamilton, Herkimer, Lewis, Oneida, Saratoga, St. Lawrence, Sullivan, Ulster, Warren and Washington. NYC's outdoor barbecue rules cover Bronx, Kings, New York, Queens and Richmond. The statewide residential brush-burning ban runs March 16 through May 14. [DEC open burning](https://dec.ny.gov/environmental-protection/air-quality/open-burning) · [NYC 311 barbecue rules](https://portal.311.nyc.gov/article/?kanumber=KA-02228)
- **Sources and stack.** The Drupal DEC and WebForms NYC 311 adapters use shared HTTP, HTML and Eastern calendar helpers. Both poll daily in positive-only mode with recurring metadata. The calendar now preserves distinct town scopes when several rules map to one county. The DEC adapter rechecks the rule text and Fire Town list and reports the permit requirement only during the brush-burning exception window. [Exact captures and mapping](../src/worker/sources/ny/fixtures/README.md)
- **Emergency and local orders.** The Governor issues statewide or regional proclamations under ECL § 9-1101. Local chief executives can issue emergency orders under Executive Law § 24, and local ordinances and land-manager rules can be stricter. The October 2025 statewide order expired October 15, the Governor's search misses a known 2024 order, and Ulster's order section had no dated entries. DEC's November 24 fire-danger table describes weather risk. NYC Parks and NYS Health returned CloudFront 403, and the Senate statute pages returned Cloudflare 403. For current orders, check DEC and Governor notices, the local fire department or emergency manager, and the land manager for the burn site. [Governor's pressroom](https://www.governor.ny.gov/news) · [DEC advisories](https://dec.ny.gov/news/press-releases?text=burn%20ban) · [Local authority law](https://www.dhses.ny.gov/nys-executive-law-article-2-b) · [Ulster orders](https://www.ulstercountyny.gov/Departments/Emergency-Services/Burn-Bans)
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge published both sources at 7:56 a.m. Eastern. Coverage, the active list, all 62 county API responses and the Clinton, Kings and Albany county pages agreed. Bun passed 324 tests, TypeScript passed and the site built. Fixture p95 wall times were 0.605 ms for DEC and 0.221 ms for NYC 311. The combined fixture test passed its 4 ms p95 thread CPU limit. The existing repository-wide benchmarks reached 14.476 ms for the legacy merge and 10.229 ms for the 51-area projection, above the 7 ms review threshold. The link check had 231 OK, 26 access blocks and one failure, the existing Arkansas Forestry TLS certificate chain. NYC Parks was the only blocked New York registry link.

## 2026-11-24 Virginia (VA)

- **Restrictions.** Shenandoah National Park restricted open solid-fuel fires beginning November 21 at 8 a.m. Eastern. Its alert covers parts of Albemarle, Augusta, Greene, Madison, Page, Rappahannock, Rockingham and Warren. Rapidan WMA's separate order began at the same time and covers parts of Greene and Madison. The morning source check found eight restricted county equivalents, 125 unknown, zero countywide bans and zero all-clears. The statewide spring rule was outside its season. [NPS conditions](https://www.nps.gov/shen/planyourvisit/conditions.htm) · [Rapidan WMA](https://dwr.virginia.gov/wma/rapidan/)
- **Sources and authority.** The hourly NPS CommonSpot JSON embed and DWR WordPress notice table publish only affirmative land-manager restrictions. The NPS report expires at the alert's display end, and both fetchers reject old cached responses. The daily Virginia LIS HTML source checks the text of the 4 p.m. law and applies the February 15–April 30 Eastern calendar within its 300-foot and jurisdiction limits. The recurring registry flag suppresses scheduled alerts. The Governor can proclaim emergency restrictions on the State Forester's recommendation. Counties, independent cities and towns can set local limits, and state air-quality rules apply separately. [Spring statute](https://law.lis.virginia.gov/vacode/title10.1/chapter11/section10.1-1142/) · [Emergency statute](https://law.lis.virginia.gov/vacode/title10.1/chapter11/section10.1-1158/) · [Air-quality rules](https://law.lis.virginia.gov/admincodeexpand/title9/agency5/chapter130/)
- **Manual checks.** DOF's WordPress REST search returned 403 and its historical ArcGIS layer requires a token. DEQ and Bedford returned Akamai 403s. Loudoun's notices give standing seasonal and location rules. Fairfax and Prince William publish permits and activity-specific rules. Ask the local fire marshal or emergency manager for current orders at the address and check the land manager for restrictions at the burn site. [DOF guidance](https://www.dof.virginia.gov/wildland-prescribed-fire/fire-laws/local-burn-bans/) · [All inventory checks and dated captures](../src/worker/sources/va/fixtures/README.md)
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge published all three automated sources at 8:11 a.m. Eastern. Coverage, the active list, all 133 county API responses, and the Madison and Albemarle pages agreed. Bun passed 331 tests, TypeScript passed, and the site built. Fixture p95 was 0.427 ms for the statute, 0.150 ms for Rapidan and 0.125 ms for Shenandoah. The combined full responses used 3.914 ms p95 thread CPU. The existing aggregate benchmarks reached 13.022 and 10.534 ms p95 against the 7 ms review threshold. The full link check had 244 OK, 20 access blocks and three failures. The two Tennessee pages returned 200 on direct retry. Arkansas Forestry's TLS certificate-chain failure reproduced. The DEQ link was the only shipped Virginia access block on that run.

## VA county pass (2026-11-24)

The November 24 fixture merge moved **53 of 125** previously unknown localities to a status. Brunswick was the one countywide ban. Of the 52 new restricted summaries, 51 came from standing local rules and one from Nottoway's scoped active ban. Virginia totals were **1 ban, 60 restricted, 72 unknown and 0 all-clears**. The Rockingham County permit rule overlaps the earlier Shenandoah National Park coverage. [Brunswick notices](https://www.brunswickco.com/cms/One.aspx?pageId=10858888&portalId=10858880) · [Nottoway County News](https://nottoway.org/)

Two hourly adapters read the CivicLive public notice and Revize homepage cards. Both use positive-only coverage. Brunswick gave an effective time. Nottoway gave a November 20 publication date and left geographic boundaries unspecified, so its ban stays scoped. The shared local calendar helper checks the reviewed HTML region or PDF digest before applying 53 recurring rules across 52 localities. Daily polls are staggered across Eastern hours. City rules use the independent city's own FIPS. Towns, campus property and fire-district areas map to the covered portion. [Shared helper](../src/worker/sources/lib/local-calendar.ts) · [Requests and captures](../src/worker/sources/va/fixtures/README.md#county-pass-captures)

The sweep used the Commonwealth's official directory to check all 133 county equivalents. It searched fire authorities, board records and public alert, news and permit channels. The ArcGIS public catalog supplied no current statewide county-order layer. The Isle of Wight ArcGIS app maps development districts. The indexed Albemarle map reports individual burns. AlertSense exposed readable Greene, Alleghany and Hopewell pages. Everbridge and Smart911 pages checked were subscriptions. The Grayson Nixle link returned HTTP 404. [Local government directory](https://www.virginia.gov/local-government/) · [Local emergency managers](https://lemd.vdem.virginia.gov/public/) · [Isle of Wight district map](https://iowgis.maps.arcgis.com/apps/instant/sidebar/index.html?appid=cbde47ae0eab4f158b99d1f7f64aaa78) · [Greene alerts](https://page.alertsense.com/content/2182/) · [Grayson Nixle link](https://www.graysoncountyva.gov/nixle)

## 2026-11-24 West Virginia (WV)

- **Restrictions.** All 55 counties have a restriction on covered outdoor burning from 7 a.m. to 5 p.m. Eastern on November 24. During that window the counts are 55 restricted, zero bans, zero all-clears and zero unknown summaries. Emergency and local order status remains unconfirmed for all 55. The seasonal rule runs March 1–May 31 and October 1–December 31. It has exceptions for certain small attended fires, snow cover and permits. [Forestry guidance](https://wvforestry.com/fire-laws/) · [October 2026 confirmation](https://ready.wv.gov/article/wv-fall-fire-season-oct-1-dec-31)
- **Source and stack.** Forestry's WordPress REST page 119 contains Divi HTML. The adapter checks the published page and reviewed seasonal language daily at midnight Eastern using shared WordPress, HTTP, HTML and calendar helpers. The shared WordPress block parser also serves the two Colorado REST adapters. This source is positive-only and marked recurring, so changes at the clock boundaries do not send alerts. [Exact capture and inventory checks](../src/worker/sources/wv/fixtures/README.md)
- **Manual checks.** The Forestry director can prohibit burning in designated areas with the Governor's approval. The current Governor archive had no recent order and the Secretary of State search returned HTTP 500 on submission. The Legislature site timed out on direct GET, and the Fire Marshal's statute PDF was available. The 2019 Commerce and 2024 Governor releases ended past statewide orders. Fayette County's 2024 minutes record a separate county order and lift. Berkeley's guidance and PDF describe standing rules, and its alerts showed no current order. For a specific address or activity, check Forestry, the county or city, the land manager and DEP's air-quality rules. [Forestry authority](https://code.wvlegislature.gov/19-1A-3/) · [Fire season and emergency statute](https://code.wvlegislature.gov/20-3-5/) · [Executive Journal](https://apps.sos.wv.gov/adlaw/executivejournal/) · [Fayette County minutes](https://fayettecounty.wv.gov/commission/SiteAssets/Pages/Minutes%20and%20Previous%20Agendas/All%20Minutes%202024.pdf) · [County contacts](https://emd.wv.gov/document-search/west-virginia-local-emergency-management-contact-information) · [DEP](https://dep.wv.gov/DAQ/CANDE/Pages/default.aspx)
- **Verification.** The local hourly cron and delayed merge published at 8:21 a.m. Eastern. Coverage, the active list, all 55 county API responses and the Berkeley County page agreed. Bun passed 336 tests, TypeScript passed and the site built. The fixture took 0.582 ms p95 wall time and 2.202 ms p95 thread CPU. The repository-wide merge and synthetic 51-area projection measured 13.296 and 10.259 ms p95 against the existing 7 ms review threshold. Every shipped West Virginia link passed. The sitewide link check reported 244 OK, 28 access blocks and one failure on the existing Arkansas Forestry feed's TLS certificate chain.

## 2026-11-24 Maryland (MD)

- **Restrictions.** On November 24, 23 counties had scoped woodland restrictions and Baltimore City was unknown. There were zero verified emergency bans and zero all-clears. DNR's year-round rule covers burning in or near woodland outside incorporated towns. Its ordinary 4 p.m.–midnight hours have snow and permit exceptions. The separate June 1–August 31 air-quality rule covers 11 counties and Baltimore City and was out of season. [DNR guidance](https://dnr.maryland.gov/forests/Pages/fire/prevention-burning-guidance.aspx) · [Summer regulation](https://regs.maryland.gov/us/md/exec/comar/26.11.07.03#C)
- **Sources and stack.** The rendered DNR SharePoint guidance and the Division of State Documents' bulk HTML both poll daily in positive-only mode. They verify the reviewed rule text and use shared HTTP, HTML and Eastern calendar helpers. The SharePoint and HTML helpers now support requiring a unique heading. Both sources carry recurring metadata so scheduled changes skip alerts. [Exact captures and all 11 inventory checks](../src/worker/sources/md/fixtures/README.md)
- **Emergency and local orders.** The DNR Secretary or Governor can issue emergency bans under §5-720. The Forest Service director has separate authority for woodland bans, and local issuers can impose local orders. DNR's WordPress search showed the 2024 statewide order and lift. Worcester's latest burn-ban notice was its July 27, 2026 lift. Wicomico's spring PDFs and rolling RSS did not establish current status. The DNR SharePoint REST query returned 401 and the Prince George's statewide-order mirror returned Akamai 403. Check DNR announcements and the address's fire marshal or local health department for current orders and permits. [State authority](https://mgaleg.maryland.gov/2026RS/Statute_Web/gnr/5-720.pdf) · [DNR announcements](https://news.maryland.gov/dnr/?s=burn+ban) · [Worcester Fire Marshal](https://worcestermd.gov/departments/fmo/fmo-news) · [Local health departments](https://health.maryland.gov/Pages/departments.aspx)
- **Verification.** The isolated local hourly cron dispatched both Maryland polls. The delayed merge published at 8:36 a.m. Eastern, and coverage, the active list and all 24 county-level API responses agreed. Bun passed 342 tests and TypeScript passed. The fixture wall times were 0.311 and 0.443 ms p95, and the combined fixture thread CPU was 2.093 ms p95. The existing aggregate benchmark warnings remained at 14.766 and 10.282 ms p95. Every shipped Maryland link passed. The sitewide link check reported 252 OK, 27 access blocks and one failure on the existing Arkansas Forestry feed's TLS certificate chain.

## 2026-11-24 New Mexico (NM)

- **Restrictions.** All 33 county summaries are restricted by standing open-burning rules in the applicable air-quality jurisdiction. There are zero verified countywide bans, zero all-clears and zero unknown summaries. The State Parks portal adds campfire restrictions at 16 parks across Colfax, Doña Ana, Luna, Mora, Otero, Rio Arriba, Roosevelt, San Juan, San Miguel, Sandoval, Santa Fe and Torrance. Angel Fire lifted its temporary village ban September 21 and retains permit and inspection rules for applicable fires. Its shorter No Burn Days require a local check. [State parks](https://wwwapps.emnrd.nm.gov/SPD/ParksReportingPublicDisplay/Restriction/Index) · [Angel Fire](https://www.angelfirenm.gov/182/Current-Fire-Restrictions)
- **Authority and stack.** The State Forester rescinded the April 6 restrictions for non-federal, non-tribal and non-municipal lands on September 28. Counties, municipalities, tribes and federal land managers can restrict fire within their jurisdictions. The ASP.NET MVC park listing uses the shared HTTP, HTML grid and date helpers. The CivicPlus village page uses a shared content-widget reader. Both administrative-code feeds use the legacy HTML section reader, Windows-1252 decoding and the shared Mountain calendar. These three standing rule sources carry recurring metadata to suppress alerts. The state air rule excludes Bernalillo and tribal lands. Bernalillo uses its own air-quality code. Totals are one restricted, zero bans, zero all-clears and zero unknown county summaries. Recreation and a few other activities may qualify for limited exceptions. Rock Creek Park has a separate restriction for fires and grills on NPS land it manages within DC. [DOEE rule](https://doee.dc.gov/sites/default/files/dc/sites/ddoe/publication/attachments/aqd.revch6_.pdf#page=4) · [Rock Creek compendium](https://www.nps.gov/rocr/learn/management/rock-creek-park-superintendent-compendium.htm)
- **Sources and stack.** DCRegs ASP.NET WebForms lists the latest rule and links to the reviewed Word download. The shared HTML grid reader now supports a direct download URL published apart from the link action. NPS CommonSpot provides the park's approved compendium. Both adapters use shared HTTP, HTML, hash and Eastern calendar helpers and poll daily as recurring, positive-only sources. A new rule document or compendium revision requires a scope and terms review. [Captures and all six inventory checks](../src/worker/sources/dc/fixtures/README.md)
- **Manual checks.** All six inventory URLs responded. The checked FEMS and AlertDC notices did not establish temporary District-wide status. The Mayor can issue air-pollution emergency orders, and the Rock Creek Superintendent can order a park fire ban. Check with the Fire Marshal about permits and current limits for the planned burn. Check park alerts and the land manager's rules for the site. [FEMS permits](https://fems.dc.gov/service/operational-permits) · [AlertDC](https://hsema.dc.gov/featured-content/alert-dc-emap) · [Rock Creek conditions](https://www.nps.gov/rocr/planyourvisit/conditions.htm)
- **Verification.** The isolated local Worker replayed the 9:07 a.m. Eastern cron slot and published both sources after the two-minute queue delay. The coverage API, active list, FIPS 11001 lookup and county page agreed. Bun passed 453 tests, TypeScript passed and the site built. Both fixture parsers passed the 4 ms p95 thread CPU tests. Fixture wall p95 was 0.86 ms for DCRegs and 0.99 ms for Rock Creek. The aggregate benchmark flagged 18.35 ms for the current-source merge and 11.52 ms for the 51-area projection. All DC URLs passed. The sitewide link check reported 364 OK, 33 blocked and six failures. The Nevada, Oregon, Tennessee and both Virginia URLs returned HTTP 200 on direct retry. The Arkansas Forestry certificate-chain error reproduced.

### Indiana and Miami-Dade standing residential rules (2026-11-24)

- Indiana IDEM exempts some residential open burning but excludes Clark, Floyd, Lake and Porter counties. The daily check verifies the listed counties and the residential activity definition. [IDEM guidance](https://www.in.gov/idem/openburning/exempt-burning-activities-and-required-conditions)
- Miami-Dade Fire Rescue prohibits residential yard debris burning and provides separate bonfire permit guidance. The daily check verifies the prohibition in the county's permit guidance. [Fire Rescue guidance](https://www.miamidade.gov/global/permit.page?Mduid_permit=per1518193325458214)

## IA county pass (2026-11-24)

- **Map result.** All 99 counties were checked. The registry and merge produced 29 restricted, one `none` (Cedar), zero bans and 69 unknown. Four county bulletins reported a countywide lift or no active restrictions. Decatur, Mills and Pottawattamie also have narrower standing rules, which set their map summaries to restricted. Of the 69 unconfirmed counties, 25 had a historical item, guidance, referral or human-only account, and 44 had no separate signal in the channels checked. [County bulletins](../src/worker/sources/ia/county-status.ts), [standing rules](../src/worker/sources/ia/local-rules.ts), [requests and fixtures](../src/worker/sources/ia/fixtures/README.md).
- **State authority and completeness.** Iowa Code § 100.40 authorizes the State Fire Marshal to issue area-specific proclamations after a local request and investigation. The requesting authority can rescind after notifying the marshal. The Nov. 24 ArcGIS response contained 99 unique county rows, all with null status, request, proclamation and lift fields. The layer last changed on April 27, 2026. The dashboard and web map recognize `yes` for active and `lift` for lifted. The feed gives no maintained `No` value, so the state adapter uses positive-only mode. A county point also lacks the geographic detail supplied in the signed order, as the Woodbury fire district proclamation shows. [Iowa Code](https://www.legis.iowa.gov/docs/code/100.40.pdf), [public dashboard](https://iowa.maps.arcgis.com/apps/dashboards/c22956334fd8435080ed851f549c5263), [layer](https://services.arcgis.com/vPD5PVLI6sfkZ5E4/arcgis/rest/services/Active_Burn_Bans_%28View%29/FeatureServer/0), [dashboard configuration](https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/c22956334fd8435080ed851f549c5263/data?f=json), [Woodbury proclamation](https://dps.iowa.gov/media/2623/download?inline=).
- **Local decisions.** Cedar's live banner shows a March 6 lift, and its separate EMA site also displays a current no-ban message. Decatur's current panel records the February request and March 3 signed lift. Mills still displays its April 2 signed lift in the active Alert Center and on its site-wide banner. Pottawattamie posts current fire conditions and its EMA coordinates countywide issues and lifts with the fire chiefs. Its May 2025 district order ended June 2, 2025. The three dated lifts count while the publishers carry them in their current status panels or active alert list. Missing and ambiguous decisions leave the countywide result unknown. [Cedar EMA](https://ema.cedar-county.org/), [Decatur signed lift](https://decaturcountyia.municipalone.com/document/50372), [Mills signed lift](https://www.millscountyiowa.gov/DocumentCenter/View/1675/Mills-County-Lifts-Open-Burning-Ban), [Pottawattamie policy](https://www.pottcounty-ia.gov/files/emergency_management/burn_ban_policy_65739.pdf), [district lift](https://www.pottcounty-ia.gov/news/pottawattamie_county_burn_ban_lifted/).
- **Polling.** The four county bulletins poll hourly using Neapolitan Labs, Municipal One and CivicPlus. The 25 recurring rules poll once daily at 4 a.m. Central. The shared calendar supports county-specific scope and links. Three larger reviewed PDF codes use strong ETags on HEAD responses, and CivicPlus alert parsing reads the posting timestamp. [Reviewed local calendar](../src/worker/sources/lib/local-calendar.ts), [CivicPlus helper](../src/worker/sources/lib/civicplus.ts).
- **Additional channels.** The sweep followed both government directories, county EMA and ordinance pages, sitemaps, CivicPlus Alert Center and RSS metadata, and official issue and lift notices. Alert Iowa and Fairfax's Everbridge page offer subscription or account forms. No public countywide burn status feed surfaced in Alert Iowa, Everbridge or Nixle. The Pottawattamie ArcGIS fire-district layer publishes geography. Polk's current issued permits list individual authorizations. Davenport publishes its daily burn decision on its phone line. [County directory](https://www.iowa.gov/local-government), [EMA directory](https://homelandsecurity.iowa.gov/resources/county-emergency-management), [Alert Iowa](https://homelandsecurity.iowa.gov/programs/alert-iowa), [Fairfax signup](https://member.everbridge.net/index/76486388220144), [district layer](https://gis.pottcounty-ia.gov/arcgis/rest/services/PCSO_Dispatch_Map_MIL/MapServer/43), [Polk issued permits](https://www.polkcountyiowa.gov/public-works/air-quality/open-burning/current-issued-permits/), [Davenport burn line](https://www.davenportiowa.com/government/departments/fire/fire_services).
- **Access and follow-up.** Curl received 403 on Polk's daily decision and permit pages, Linn's county health program, Scott EMA's notice archive, and Guthrie's burn-ban page. Dallas's services index was blocked and its EMA response page was reachable. West Des Moines's permit page and the Mason City and Waterloo American Legal pages also returned 403. Fort Dodge's legacy guidance URL and Muscatine's draft county PDF returned 404. The old Union domain was blocked and Emmet's directory hostname failed to resolve. The newer county sites responded. Confirm the Oct. 12 Greene County emergency ban report with the Sheriff or EMA. The sweep found the standing county ordinance and no matching current signed order or lift. Check the four bulletin formats at the next issuance, especially the geographic scope in the posted order. [Polk daily page](https://www.polkcountyiowa.gov/public-works/air-quality/open-burning/can-i-burn-today/), [Linn public health](https://www.linncountyiowa.gov/1530/Open-Burning), [Scott EMA archive](https://www.scottcountyiowa.gov/ema/news?items_per_page=60&page=0), [Guthrie EMA](https://guthriecounty.gov/emergency_management/open_burn_ban_information/), [Greene Sheriff](https://www.greenecounty.iowa.gov/pview.aspx?catid=661&id=15462), [Oct. 12 report](https://www.raccoonvalleyradio.com/2026/10/12/greene-county-is-now-under-a-burn-ban-3/).
- **Verification.** All 30 Iowa registry requests returned 200. Replaying the complete responses through the actual pollers and county merge confirmed the counts above. All 503 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. Iowa fixture parsers stayed under 5 ms p95 process CPU, and the slowest Iowa fixture in the wall-time benchmark was 2.26 ms p95. The link checker reported 569 OK, 33 blocked and three failures. The existing Arkansas AgTools URL failed TLS verification, Oregon DEQ timed out and Tennessee's permit page reset the Bun connection. All Iowa registry URLs passed.

## WI county pass (2026-11-24)

- **Map result.** With the recorded local sources alone, 67 of 72 counties show restricted, all with a specific local or activity scope. Five remain unknown. The captured local feeds produce zero current countywide bans and zero countywide all-clears. The two explicit local clear flags do not establish status for the rest of their counties. Research crossed Central midnight and fixtures record their actual capture dates. [Fixture index](../src/worker/sources/wi/fixtures/README.md).
- **Live publishers.** Burlington’s Juniper panel and Somerset’s WordPress/Elementor permit page report their own local bans. Somerset includes both alternatives in the HTML and marks one hidden. The Town of Clinton WordPress API mirrors county Emergency Management orders and matching lift notices for Vernon. Menomonee Falls publishes village Fire Chief notices in its CivicPlus Safety Strategies RSS and News Flash. The four sources are positive-only. The order parsers verify the jurisdiction and effective date and age out old notices. [Town of Burlington](https://www.townofburlingtonwi.gov/fire-department/) · [Somerset Fire & Rescue](https://somersetfirerescue.com/burning-permits/) · [Vernon order search](https://tn.clinton.wi.gov/?s=burn+ban) · [Menomonee Falls News Flash](https://www.menomoneefalls.gov/CivicAlerts.aspx).
- **Standing rules.** The 67 recurring sources use the existing local calendar and HTML/PDF review helpers. Covered activities include permits, notification, operating hours, debris burning, and tribal camping fires. Each report names the affected municipality, unincorporated area, tribal land or activity. The scheduled checks suspend changed rules pending review. The statewide WisBURN adapter still handles its separate daily decisions. [Local rule registry](../src/worker/sources/wi/local-rules.ts) · [Wisconsin ordinance directory](https://wilawlibrary.gov/topics/ordinances.php).
- **Other publishing channels.** The sweep found no independent state per-county local-order map or KML. Fire response boundary maps describe jurisdiction. The public First Due Community Connect page for Somerset offers permit access and guidance with no public daily flag. Vernon Everbridge and Green County emergency alerts offer sign-ups. Green County’s CivicPlus RSS had no matching burn order. Onalaska and Greenfield preserve historical releases. The formal notice area can be narrower than the county named in a DNR danger advisory. [Brown County fire response areas](https://gis-docs.browncountywi.gov/web_documents/LIO/PDF/FireResponseAreasStreetMap42wide_Portrait.pdf) · [Somerset permits portal](https://www.communityconnect.io/info/wi-somerset) · [Vernon Everbridge](https://member.everbridge.net/index/101603356966946) · [Green County emergency alerts](https://www.greencountywi.org/638/Emergency-Alerts) · [Shelby notices in the Greenfield archive](https://www.greenfieldlaxwi.gov/government/notices/?pg=2).
- **Human-only links.** The government publishers link to these accounts for local updates. The accounts were excluded from collection. [Burlington Fire](https://www.facebook.com/TOBFD) · [Vernon County Emergency Management](https://www.facebook.com/VernCoWIEM/) · [Monroe Fire](https://www.facebook.com/MonroeFireWI/) · [Crandon Police](https://www.facebook.com/CrandonPolice/) · [Shell Lake Fire](https://facebook.com/shelllakefiredepartment).
- **Blocked or unavailable candidates.** Curl returned 403 for the Milwaukee Fire advisory, Kenosha city code, Sheboygan city rules, La Crosse and Monroe County Emergency Management, Medford’s American Legal code and Vernon’s Municode supplier. The older Onalaska Revize release URLs returned 404. The active rules below link to verified alternatives. [Milwaukee advisory](https://city.milwaukee.gov/ImageLibrary/Groups/mfdAuthors/media/2024/Wildfire-Informational-Warning-10.29.24.pdf) · [Kenosha city code](https://www.kenosha.org/images/GENORD.pdf) · [Sheboygan city guidance](https://www.sheboyganwi.gov/598/Open-Burning-Information) · [La Crosse County EMA](https://lacrossecounty.org/sheriff/sheriff-home/divisions/emergencymanagement) · [Monroe County EMA](https://www.co.monroe.wi.us/departments/emergency-management) · [Medford code](https://codelibrary.amlegal.com/codes/medford/latest/medford_wi/0-0-0-7126) · [Vernon code](https://library.municode.com/wi/vernon_county/codes/code_of_ordinances?nodeId=CO_CH31HESANVSA_ARTXIIOUREBU).

**Verification.** The registered Wisconsin sources replayed all 72 county lookups from captured publisher responses. The 67 scoped restrictions produced zero alert transitions. All 652 Bun tests and TypeScript passed. The maximum Wisconsin parser p95 was 4.52 ms in the fixture benchmark. All Wisconsin registry and directory links passed. The sitewide checker reported 770 successful links, 36 access blocks and eight failures. Idaho’s IDL ArcGIS service and three DEQ URLs, the Ames FAQ and the Lincoln environmental air page timed out. Arkansas’s forestry endpoint failed TLS verification. Tennessee’s seasonal permit page reset the connection.

## MI county pass (2026-11-24)

**Map impact.** Replaying the documented November 24 DNR baseline with the new local sources moves 49 counties from unknown to scoped restricted. The resulting fixture map has 59 restricted, 24 unknown, zero countywide bans and zero countywide all-clears. The workstation captured the local publisher responses on November 25. Standing rules and the open-ended Spencer order were replayed only where their terms cover the baseline date. The new Chesterfield weather capture was checked on its own publication date.

**Local decisions.** Chesterfield's public WordPress JSON exposes an administrative flag and a weather gate from the permit form. The fixture shows a weather hold. An Eastern check time, the station observation and their expiry bound the weather result. Spencer's Elementor permit section still displays its July 14 permit suspension until further notice. Vernon's July 13 ban is hidden across device sizes. The shared Elementor reader drops hidden widgets. East Lansing's CivicPlus RSS is a current listing, with a second check that a selected article is still active and matches the city and publication date. The current feed contains no qualifying order. [Chesterfield](https://chesterfieldfire.org/how-do-i/request-an-open-burn-permit/) · [Spencer Township](https://spencertwpmi.gov/fire/) · [Vernon Township](https://vernontwp-mi.gov/fire-department/) · [East Lansing current news](https://www.cityofeastlansing.com/m/newsflash) · [archived 2023 order](https://www.cityofeastlansing.com/CivicAlerts.aspx?AID=1906&ARC=4134).

**Standing rules.** The 42 recurring sources use the reviewed local-calendar helper with HTML blocks, ordinance bytes or strong PDF validators. Chesterfield's off-season dates complement its live flag. Huron-Manistee Order R904-18-02 is listed in the Forest Service's current alerts without an end date. It limits fires near water or designated trails in dispersed areas on federal forest land. The 14 mapped counties come from the Forest Service plan. The 2024 acreage table also lists isolated parcels of 10 acres or fewer outside the plan's county roster. [Forest order](https://www.fs.usda.gov/r09/huron-manistee/alerts/occupancy-and-use-restrictions) · [current forest alerts](https://www.fs.usda.gov/r09/huron-manistee/alerts) · [Forest Service plan and county roster](https://www.fs.usda.gov/Internet/FSE_DOCUMENTS/stelprdb5442524.pdf) · [Forest Service acreage table](https://www.fs.usda.gov/land/staff/lar/LAR2024/LARTable6.pdf) · [fixture requests and capture times](../src/worker/sources/mi/fixtures/county-pass/README.md).

**Coverage gaps and human routes.** Local issuers still handle the gray DNR areas. The state fire directory lists departments by county and the townships association supplies municipal contacts. The Allegan dispatch list and other county guidance can route callers to the local issuer. The Oakland BurnPermit.us portals defer day-of decisions to telephone activation. Livingston's county notification system and Imlay City's Nixle link offer subscriptions. Cedar Springs has a dated AlertSense issuance from June 2026 and a subscription page. A current issuer decision or lift could not be verified from those pages. [Fire Service Directory](https://fireservicedirectory.apps.lara.state.mi.us/) · [township directory](https://michigantownships.org/find-a-township/) · [Allegan dispatch](https://www.allegancounty.org/departments/central-dispatch/burn-permits) · [Oakland NOCFA permit portal](https://nocfa.burnpermit.us/) · [Livingston alerts](https://milivcounty.gov/emergency-management/livco-alert-system/) · [Imlay City Nixle](https://www.imlaycity.org/departments-services/police-department/nixle-information/) · [June Cedar Springs alert](https://page.alertsense.com/content/2420/163848).

**Human-only social links.** The official Monitor and Livingston pages point to these accounts for local updates. [Monitor Township Fire on Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/monitortwpfire) · [Livingston Emergency Management on Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/livcoem/) · [Livingston Emergency Management on X](https://twitter.com/LivCoEM).

**Verification.** The working tree passed all 680 Bun tests and TypeScript. A checkout of the staged files passed all 666 Bun tests and TypeScript. In the standalone fixture benchmark the slowest new Michigan parser was Gladwin at 3.92 ms p95. The staged Michigan fixtures also passed their individual timing budgets. The sitewide link check reported 920 OK, 31 access blocks and three failures across 954 URLs. The newly registered Michigan URLs passed. Curl reproduced the Arkansas Forestry certificate failure and the Tennessee burn-ban page's connection reset. The Tennessee permit page returned 200 on retry.

**Lead review.** Sixteen remaining unknown counties have scoped sources in the earlier draft. Review that draft separately. Confirm Spencer's still-visible July permit suspension with its issuer before rollout.

## CA county pass (2026-11-24)

The host and publishers were on November 25 Pacific time during capture. The November 25 fixture replay moved all 58 counties from unknown to **restricted** through the normal merge. Local reports contributed active restrictions in 21 counties. Standing rules supplied the remaining 37. All results retain activity and jurisdiction. The replay had zero countywide bans and zero countywide all-clears. [Fixture provenance and request URLs](../src/worker/sources/ca/fixtures/county/README.md)

The pass adds 15 monitored decision sources and 24 recurring rule sources. ArcGIS supplies Placer's residential zones and South Coast's residential wood-burning designation. Valley Air publishes residential and hazard-reduction decisions in one county JSON feed. Mendocino uses WordPress REST, and Sacramento's SharePoint widget links to dated S3 season history. The other sources publish HTML notices, widgets or tables. Streamline powers Butte, Feather River and Portola. The shared daily-decision helper requires explicit dates, expires rows at Pacific midnight and omits permissive and pending decisions. Reviewed rules use the existing calendar and evidence fingerprints. [Local air district directory](https://ww2.arb.ca.gov/california-air-districts)

The CARB directory links to local publishers. A public ArcGIS search surfaced prescribed-burn inventories and a 2018 permit form, alongside the local operational maps found through agency embeds. No additional statewide county order roster surfaced. The South Coast and Santa Barbara Web AppBuilder maps expose operational layers. Santa Barbara's decision is represented by the web map's layer visibility, and the item carrying those toggles was last modified September 21, 2026. Placer and El Dorado publish complete scoped decision rosters. All 15 adapters are positive-only because their results cover particular activities or areas. [CARB local status directory](https://ww2.arb.ca.gov/smoke-management-programs-and-burn-decisions-other-air-districts) · [ArcGIS search](https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/search?f=json&q=California%20%28%22burn%20ban%22%20OR%20%22burn%20status%22%20OR%20%22burn%20day%22%29&num=30) · [Santa Barbara web map item](https://sbc-gis.maps.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/922eb6ec504d401c9bd5bfbddee02f0b?f=json)

Human-only channels include the official El Dorado, Glenn and South Coast district social accounts. The Bay Area's current alert page showed no alert. Its November 24 Thanksgiving request was voluntary. The live alert presentation does not establish an automated particulate-specific ban in this pass. The existing statewide CAL FIRE URL still returned Akamai 403 in the repository link check. [El Dorado Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/EDCAQMD) · [El Dorado X](https://x.com/EDCAQMD) · [Glenn X](https://x.com/GlennCountyAir) · [South Coast Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/SouthCoastAQMD) · [Bay Area alert status](https://www.baaqmd.gov/en/alertstatus) · [November 24 Bay Area notice](https://www.baaqmd.gov/en/News-and-Events/Page-Resources/2026-News/112426-Voluntary-No-Burn)

## SD county pass (2026-11-24)

- The sweep checked all 66 counties. Replaying the county responses and the previous SD fixtures moved 21 counties out of unknown. Eighteen moved to restricted and three to none. State totals were 28 restricted, three none, zero bans and 35 unknown. Davison, Spink and Yankton publish explicit current countywide decisions. Sully posts a live banner for its ordinance area outside municipalities and planned unit developments. The recurring rules below cover activities within their stated jurisdictions, including the portions of Sioux Falls in Lincoln and Minnehaha counties. Captures were made November 25–26 Central time for the November 24 pass. [Fixture requests and capture times](../src/worker/sources/sd/fixtures/county-pass/README.md)
- Four county bulletins poll five times per day Central time through a shared county bulletin helper. Yankton’s dated decision expires from the map after seven local days without a new date. Eighteen recurring rules poll once daily. The calendar helper verifies reviewed HTML sections, PDF bytes or strong document validators before extending the rule. [County bulletins](../src/worker/sources/sd/county-status.ts) · [Local rules](../src/worker/sources/sd/local-rules.ts)
- The Local Government Boards and Commissions portal is a meeting-document archive with uneven county participation. The Rocky Mountain dashboard's county layer is for reference. Its restriction layers cover BIA, NPS, FWS, BLM, Wyoming and the Black Hills district. The BIA query returned eight SD agency areas with `None`, no linked orders and the same April 30 edit date. Those are reservation and agency records, with incomplete county attributes. The NRCS packet's statewide county maps are from 2015. No current official statewide county register or public Google My Maps KML surfaced. [Commission portal](https://sdlocalgov.appssd.sd.gov/SearchResults.aspx?Letter=ALL) · [Dashboard web map](https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=f0a56c48c00a4adfa7ceadba4fbdebe9) · [BIA service](https://services3.arcgis.com/T4QMspbfLg3qTGWY/arcgis/rest/services/Rocky_Mountain_Area_BIA_Fire_Restriction_Polygons_VIEW/FeatureServer/0) · [Historical maps](https://www.nrcs.usda.gov/sites/default/files/2022-11/Prescribed_Burning_Training_2016.pdf)
- Pennington Everbridge, Grant and Deadwood CodeRED, and Brookings civic notifications direct residents to subscribe. Lincoln's CivicPlus Alert Center and Brookings' public News Flash RSS carried no current burn decision at capture. The Sioux Falls, Aberdeen and Rapid City Rangeland Fire Danger statements report forecast categories by zone and typically publish seasonally. Several county ordinances use those categories or Red Flag Warnings. This pass reports verified current bulletins and applicable standing permit or notification terms. County-specific forecast validity, legal thresholds and split-zone boundaries need review before deriving day-of-fire-danger restrictions. [Pennington warning messages](https://www.pennco.org/law_public_safety/emergency_management/emergency_messaging_warning_systems/public_warning_messages.php) · [Brookings RSS](https://www.brookingscountysd.gov/RSSFeed.aspx?ModID=1&CID=1) · [NWS Grassland Fire Danger Index](https://www.weather.gov/unr/rfd-info)
- Bun passed all 720 tests and TypeScript passed. The registered pollers and county merge reproduced the map from the full curl responses and existing SD fixtures. The new parser benchmark peaked at 1.66 ms wall p95. The final link check recorded 1,105 OK, 39 blocked and five failures. All new SD URLs passed. Both Fall River links were blocked. Laketown's two links returned 429 and Arkansas Forestry's certificate-chain error reproduced on curl retry. Sacramento's two pages returned connection resets to Bun and HTTP 200 to curl. [Laketown search](https://laketowntwp.org/?s=burn) · [Arkansas Forestry](https://mip.agri.arkansas.gov/agtools/Forestry/Fire_Info/Burn_Bans) · [Sacramento wood burning](https://www.airquality.org/ProgramCoordination/Pages/Check-Before-You-Burn.aspx) · [Sacramento rural burning](https://www.airquality.org/residents/outdoor-burning/open-burning-(rural-yard-burning))
